New `CNodeMaskOverlay.applyMaskEdit(description, mutate, {coalesceKey, coalesce})` runs any mask mutation as one undoable action: it snapshots the full mask `ImageData` before and after, saves the mask, re-arms a render, and pushes an undo/redo pair holding both states. `clearMask()` and the new rectangle tool route through it, and the auto-mask tools (**Auto Mask OSD**, **Auto Mask Redactions**) now apply via `applyMaskEdit` from `_applyAutoMask()` with `coalesceKey: "autoMask:<name>"` — re-runs that merely replace the layer's own previous output (e.g. every tick of a slider drag) update the top undo entry's post-state instead of pushing a new one (only when the redo stack is empty), so a single undo removes the whole auto-mask application rather than one slider tick at a time.
- **Export Look Panorama now accounts for FOV (zoom) changes across the A-B export range** (Video → Video Render & Export → Export Look Panorama; `src/PanoramaExporter.js`). Frame-to-frame displacement of the tracked background point is now accumulated in radians (`getAngularDisplacement`, replacing the pixel-space `getScreenDisplacement` that baked in each frame's own FOV), and the panorama uses ONE fixed pixels-per-radian for the whole image, anchored to the narrowest (most zoomed-in) FOV in the range — so the most-zoomed frame stays at native resolution. Each frame is stamped scaled about its center by `drawScale = vFovRad / minVFovRad` to span its true angular extent (zoom is about the optical axis), and the pano bounds now include each frame's scaled half-extents. Constant-FOV exports are unchanged (`drawScale = 1`).
- **Scripted Video internals reorganized; parser now unit-tested** (`src/scriptedVideo/`). The 1900-line `src/CScriptedVideo.js` is split into focused modules — `ScriptCommands.js` (per-command parse/prepare/sample registry; adding a command is now one registry entry instead of three switch sites), `ScriptParser.js` (pure text→events, no DOM/scene access), `ScriptCameraEngine.js`, `ScriptTimelineWidget.js`, `ScriptEditorWindow.js`, `ScriptRenderer.js` — with 21 unit tests over the pure parser in `tests/scriptParser.test.js`. Minor behavior wins along the way: localStorage script saves are debounced (400 ms, `_saveScript`); when rendering with *Wait For Loading* on and motion blur off (`mbSamples === 1`), the settled view is composited directly instead of paying for a redundant second full render per frame; popping the editor out/in invalidates the cached character width; the views' `preRenderFunction` is restored verbatim after a render. Dead code removed (`tokenize`, `_renderSceneFrame`, `closestApproachTime`, `_setFullscreen`, `getScriptedVideo`).

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed dragging an info label over a 3D view also orbiting the camera** (`handlePointerDown` in `src/nodes/CNodeVideoInfoUI.js`; shared base, so both the new Sim Info labels and the existing Video Info labels benefit). The camera orbit/pan starts from a DOCUMENT-level `pointerdown` listener (`SetupMouseHandler` in mouseMoveView.js), while the label drag used a `mousedown` listener on the overlay's own canvas — `mousedown` and `pointerdown` are independent event dispatches, so the drag handler's `stopPropagation()` could never reach the camera handler, and grabbing a label also rotated the view underneath. The overlay canvas now also listens for `pointerdown`: since the canvas is a descendant of `document`, this fires at the target phase BEFORE the document's bubble-phase listener, and calling `stopPropagation()` there prevents the underlying view from ever becoming the drag view. The event is claimed only when the press actually lands on a draggable info element (`getElementAtPosition`), so the rest of the view still orbits/pans normally. The listener is removed in `dispose()`.
- **Fixed dragged info labels appearing frozen and then jumping** (`handleMouseMove` drag branch in `src/nodes/CNodeVideoInfoUI.js`). Sitrec is render-on-demand, and nothing re-armed a frame during a label drag — the overlay's position updated but nothing repainted until some other event forced a frame, so the dragged item looked stuck and then teleported. Each drag move now calls `setRenderOne(true)`, so labels follow the mouse smoothly.
- **Fixed the dead "Affine RANSAC" motion technique** (`computeAffineRANSAC` in `src/CMotionAnalysis.js`). The technique called `cv.estimateAffinePartial2D`, which the bundled `@techstark/opencv-js` build does not export, so it always fell back to Sparse + Consensus — selecting *Affine RANSAC* silently ran a different technique. The call is replaced with the available `cv.estimateAffine2D` (full 6-DOF affine + RANSAC; same 2×3 matrix layout, rotation/translation read identically) and the availability guard updated to match, so the technique now actually runs.
- **Fixed a `#` inside a quoted Scripted Video caption truncating the line** (`parseScript()` in `src/scriptedVideo/ScriptParser.js`). The comment strip was a blind `indexOf("#")`, so `text "Flight #2" 4` lost everything from the `#` on. The strip now scans for a `#` OUTSIDE double quotes, so quoted captions can contain `#` and trailing comments still work.
- **Scripted Video scripts with negative durations or unquoted multi-word captions are now parse errors** (`ScriptParser.js` / `ScriptCommands.js`). Previously a negative duration produced a malformed event on the timeline, and `text hello world 3` silently kept only the first word as the caption; both now report a per-line script error (`negative duration`, `multi-word captions need quotes`) in the editor instead of being silently mis-read.
- **Fixed scrubbing the Scripted Video timeline and then starting Preview or Render corrupting saved/restored state** (`_exitAllModes()` in `src/CScriptedVideo.js`). Preview, scrub, and render each save and restore overlapping state (camera controllers, view rects/visibility, paused flag, terrain subdivision, the bottom timeline strip); entering one mode while another was active stacked their restore closures out of order, so exiting could restore stale state. All mode entries (and `stopAll()`) now reconcile through `_exitAllModes()`, which cleanly exits preview (`stopPreview`) and scrub (`_scrubExit`) before the next mode begins.

## Version 2.79.0 (2026-06-08)

### New Features
- **KML track import now handles essentially any KML structure via a generic recursive extractor.** `src/TrackFiles/CTrackFileKML.js` replaces the old fixed six-branch shape ladder in `getKMLTrackWhenCoord` (FR24 "Route", FlightAware `Document.Placemark[2]`, ADSB-Exchange `Folder.Folder`, single-`Document`, and the `Folder>Placemark` fallback, with a dev `assert(0, "Unknown KML format…")` for anything else) with a single generic tree-walk, `extractTrackGroups()`. A recursive `visit(container, folderName)` descends `Folder` and `Document` at any depth and, for every `Placemark`, collects (time, lon, lat, alt) samples from any time+geometry pairing: `samplesFromGxTrack()` reads paired `<when>`/`<gx:coord>` (space-separated `"lon lat alt"`) from `<gx:Track>` reached directly **or** through `<gx:MultiTrack>`; `sampleFromTimedPlacemark()` reads a direct `<TimeStamp><when>` (or, newly, `<TimeSpan><begin>`) plus `<Point>` (comma-separated `"lon,lat,alt"`). Samples are grouped into tracks by enclosing-container identity (a `Map` keyed on the container object), so multiple tracks in sub-folders, and multiple data sources in one file, are all picked up; `getTrackCount()`/`hasMoreTracks()` now just report `extractTrackGroups().length`. The historical per-segment / per-sequence consecutive-duplicate-`when` dedup is preserved. The practical result is that KML variants that previously failed to import now import: `<gx:MultiTrack>`, `<TimeSpan>`-timed tracks, tracks nested in sub-folders, files containing multiple sources, FR24 single-"Route" exports with no Trail folder, FlightAware files where the track is not the third placemark (or has only two), and Sitrec's own re-imported KML exports (which formerly tripped the dev `assert`).
- **Track-name and geometry back-compat is locked across the rewrite.** A new `legacyTrackName(trackIndex)` reproduces the exact `info.name` the old shape-specific branches produced (including the `.split(" ")[0]`/`[2]` quirks) and seeds the unchanged `getShortName`, so no saved sitch keyed on `Track_<name>` is orphaned. A full-corpus before/after parity diff over ~1,380 real tracks showed zero changes to geometry, point counts, or names on the default path — the only behavioral change is that one previously-failing file now imports. The unused `assert` import was removed from `CTrackFileKML.js`. Faithfulness is enforced by `tests/CTrackFileKML.variants.test.js`: the six former `test.failing` gap fixtures now assert successful import, and a new "geometry parity" block pins track count, point count, and first/last position for representative committed fixtures so the default path can never silently drift. (See `docs/KMLDataSources.md` for the full architecture writeup.)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed held arrow keys not playing video continuously under render-on-demand** (the video viewer in particular). Arrow-key playback (←/→ at normal speed, ↑/↓ at 10×) is polled every tick inside `updateFrame()`, but render-on-demand puts the loop to sleep once `renderOne` is consumed; OS key-repeat keydowns hit the `e.repeat` early-return before `setRenderOne()`, so a held arrow advanced only a single frame (the initial keydown step) before the loop slept. The re-arm now lives in `renderMain()` in `src/indexRender.js`, placed **after** the `renderOne` clear, so it survives into the next `animate()` tick's sleep check: while any playback arrow is held, `setRenderOne(true)` keeps the loop ticking and the polling drives continuous advance; releasing the key stops the re-arm and the loop sleeps again. (Earlier sitches masked this because some node's `updateWhilePaused` work — e.g. terrain subdivision — kept the loop awake incidentally; the video viewer has no such work, which is why it exposed the bug.)

## Version 2.78.5 (2026-06-07)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed a sitch hanging in a half-loaded / "deserializing" state when it contained a 3D object whose model is a dynamic, non-registered file** (e.g. a drag-dropped model or a `.ply` Gaussian splat such as the *Splat test* sitch's `Piaggio-p180.ply`). Root cause was in `CNode3DObject.rebuild` (`src/nodes/CNode3DObject.js`): it looked the model up with `const model = ModelFiles[this.selectModel]`, but `this.selectModel` can be a model NAME (a key in the static `ModelFiles` registry) **or** a dynamic/drag-dropped FILENAME that is not registered. For the latter the lookup returned `undefined`, and the next line's `model.file` comparison threw a TypeError. That exception propagated out of `modDeserialize` and rejected the promise returned by `deserializeMods` in `src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`, so the `.then()` that runs `finishDeserialization` (and clears `Globals.deserializing`) never ran. The app was left wedged: cameras were never positioned (`finishDeserialization`'s `applyControllers` was skipped), and `deserializing`-gated behavior stayed suppressed (e.g. `markSitchDirty` early-returns while `Globals.deserializing` is true). The fix has two parts:
  - **Dynamic-model fallback** (`src/nodes/CNode3DObject.js`): the lookup is now `const model = ModelFiles[this.selectModel] ?? {file: this.selectModel}`, so an unregistered model name yields a `{file: <filename>}` def instead of `undefined`. A genuinely missing file then fails gracefully through `loadModelAsset`'s error path rather than crashing the whole load. The reload guard was changed from object-identity (`model !== this.currentModel`) to file comparison (`model.file !== this.currentModel?.file`), because the fallback is a fresh object every call — an identity check would needlessly reload the dynamic model on every rebuild.
  - **Defense-in-depth in the deserialize flow** (`src/CustomManagerSerialize.js`): `deserializeMods` now wraps each `node.modDeserialize(mods[id])` in try/catch, logging and skipping a single bad node (`Error applying mod to node "<id>" — skipping it:`) so one bad mod can never again abort the entire load. Additionally, the `deserializeMods(...).then(...)` chain gained a `.catch` that, on any unexpected throw, logs `Deserialization did not complete cleanly:`, clears `Globals.deserializing` and `Globals.sitchDirty`, and calls `setRenderOne(3)` — guaranteeing the app can never be stranded in the "deserializing" state even if a later post-mods recalc throws. The net effect: such sitches now finish loading correctly (camera positioned, object/splat rendered) instead of appearing to hang into a broken state.

## Version 2.78.4 (2026-06-07)

### Bug Fixes
- **Creating a 3D object is now undoable** (`createObjectFromInput()` in `src/CustomManagerMenus.js`). Both creation entry points — the **Objects ▸ Add Object** menu item (`src/index.js`, `menus.objects.addObject`) and the `addObjectAtLLA` API (`src/CSitrecAPI.js`) — route through `createObjectFromInput`, and neither previously pushed an undo action, so a freshly created object could not be undone, unlike synthetic buildings, clouds, and ground overlays (which already register undo in this file). `createObjectFromInput` now calls `UndoManager.add(...)` (guarded by `if (UndoManager && trackOb)`) using a new extracted helper, `makeCreateObjectUndoAction` in `src/undoCreateObject.js`. A created object is two linked nodes — a `CNode3DObject` (`objectID`) and a synthetic track (`trackID`) — so **Undo** (Ctrl/Cmd+Z) removes both: `TrackManager.disposeRemove(trackID)` first (which also tears down the track's display/data nodes), then `NodeMan.unlinkDisposeRemove(objectID)`. **Redo** (Ctrl/Cmd+Y or Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z) re-creates the pair by re-invoking `createObjectFromInput` via an injected `recreate` callback; because recreation yields fresh ids, the action stores them in `curObjectID` / `curTrackID` closure vars and updates them on redo, so a subsequent undo targets the recreated pair rather than the stale originals. The nested `UndoManager.add` during recreation is a no-op while `UndoManager.isRedoing`, so redo does not stack a duplicate action. The helper takes its managers and the `recreate` callback as injected parameters (holding no hidden globals), making it unit-testable in isolation away from `CustomManagerMenus.js`'s large import tree; covered by `tests/createObjectUndo.test.js` (7 tests).

## Version 2.78.3 (2026-06-07)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed a custom sitch hanging forever on "LOADING" when a video byte-fetch silently stalled** (root cause across `src/quickFetch.js`, `src/SitrecObjectResolver.js`, `src/CFileManager.js`, `src/CVideoMp4Data.js`). A browser `fetch` on a half-open / silently-dropped connection never resolves *and* never rejects. `CFileManager.loadAsset` → `fileSystemFetch` → `quickFetch` downloads large files as many independent S3 range requests (a 43 MB `.mov` ≈ 23 chunk fetches), so a single stalled chunk wedged the whole load at 0% forever: the "LOADING" overlay never cleared, the app never reached "no pending actions", and `Globals.pendingActions` / `Globals.parsing` leaked permanently. Intermittent (depends on a transient socket stall) but unrecoverable once hit, because nothing ever timed out or aborted the stalled fetch. The fix adds end-to-end stall protection so the load now succeeds, self-heals via a chunk retry, or fails recoverably (`Error Loading`) within ~10s:
  - `src/quickFetch.js`: new **inactivity** timeouts — `HEADER_TIMEOUT_MS` = 10000 (time-to-first-byte) and `STALL_TIMEOUT_MS` = 10000 (max gap between body-byte arrivals). The body timer is rearmed on *every* received byte inside the reader loop (`fetchBufferWithStall`), so a slow-but-progressing transfer is never aborted — only a true no-progress stall trips it, rejecting with a `TimeoutError` (`DOMException` name) instead of hanging. The S3 chunked path (`downloadChunk`, and the new `fetchInitialChunkWithStall` for the initial range request) retries a stalled chunk once (`STALL_RETRIES` = 1) before giving up; on final give-up `quickFetch` deliberately does **not** fall back to a plain full GET (which has no body-stall guard and would re-hang) — it re-throws so `loadAsset`'s catch can unwind. Passthrough / non-S3 / range-unsupported fallback fetches use the new `fetchWithHeaderTimeout` (header timeout only, since their body is read by the caller). New `combineSignals()` merges caller + stall `AbortSignal`s via native `AbortSignal.any` with a manual-linking fallback (older Electron runtimes). A new `progressId` option lets a caller route byte-progress to an existing LoadingManager task. All stall timers are bypassed when `Globals.regression` is set so CI stays deterministic.
  - `src/SitrecObjectResolver.js`: the `object.php` reference→URL resolve fetch — which runs *inside* `loadAsset`'s fetch chain, so a hang there pins both the "Asset" and "Video" tasks at 0% — now goes through `fetchObjectResolver()` with a `RESOLVE_TIMEOUT_MS` = 30000 timeout, honoring an optional caller `AbortSignal` (combined via `AbortSignal.any`). `resolveSitrecReference()` / `resolveURLForFetch()` gained a `signal` option threaded through to it. Regression-bypassed like the others.
  - `src/CFileManager.js`: `loadAsset(filename, id, metadataOverride, options)` gained `options.abortSignal`, linked into the internal `fetchController` (so a caller can cancel a stalled or slow load and let the catch run, decrementing `Globals.parsing` / `Globals.pendingActions` instead of leaking them). The signal is also passed into `resolveURLForFetch(fetchSource, {signal})` so a stalled `object.php` resolve is cancellable, and the Asset task's `loadingId` is passed as `fileSystemFetch`'s `progressId` so its loading row advances during a chunked download. On a `TimeoutError` the catch now logs the in-flight async ops (`asyncOperationRegistry.getPendingOperationsString()`) for field diagnostics — e.g. to show a night-sky asset burst saturating the socket pool.
  - `src/CVideoMp4Data.js`: the video data object now owns `this._fetchAbort = new AbortController()` and passes its signal to `loadAsset`. Both the 120s `getConfig` watchdog (`GET_CONFIG_TIMEOUT_MS` = 120000) and `dispose()` abort it, so a stalled or slow-but-in-flight fetch is torn down and its `pendingActions` unwind instead of leaking behind an error the user already saw. The load-failure catch was hardened to cancel the `getConfig` watchdog (`_getConfigTimer`) and set `_aborted` so it can't fire a second duplicate error 120s later, and to skip surfacing an error on an intentional `AbortError` (dispose / watchdog).

### Improvements
- **Loading panel now flags a stalled task** (`src/CLoadingManager.js`). A task showing 0% progress for at least `STALL_HINT_SECONDS` = 7s renders with an amber bar and a "⚠ stalled?" hint instead of the normal blue/teal "Starting…" row. Presentation-only (no new timer; computed from `entry.startTime` in the existing panel render), kept just under the fetch layer's stall timeout so the hint appears shortly before a stuck load auto-fails or self-recovers — and also surfaces any un-timed work that genuinely wedges.

---

## Version 2.78.2 (2026-06-07)

### Improvements
- **Auto-mask buttons now enable and open the mask in one click** (`createMaskingFolder()` in `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`, Motion Analysis → Masking). Previously, after clicking an auto-mask button you still had to manually tick **Enable Mask** and **Edit Mask** before the detected mask did anything or could be tweaked. The `autoMask` and `autoMaskRedactions` handlers now call `maskEnabledController.setValue(true)` and `editMaskController.setValue(true)` after running detection — `setValue` updates the GUI checkboxes and fires Edit Mask's `onChange` (`setMaskEditing(true)`), so a freshly detected mask is immediately active and paint-editable. This required capturing the two toggle controllers into `maskEnabledController` / `editMaskController` where they are added to the folder.
- **Renamed "Auto Mask" to "Auto Mask OSD"** (`createMaskingFolder()` in `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`, Motion Analysis → Masking). This button masks static text-coloured pixels — the burnt-in on-screen-display (OSD) telemetry overlay — and the new name disambiguates it from the sibling **Auto Mask Redactions** button, which instead targets solid redaction boxes. Behaviour is unchanged apart from the label and the new auto-enable above.
- **"Rotate Frames (SfM)" now defaults ON for motion panoramas** (Motion Analysis → Panorama → Motion Pano Options; `panoRotateFrames` default flipped `false` → `true` in `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`). With it on, the motion pano stamps each frame with its full recovered per-frame rigid transform (rotation + translation, via `calculateFrameTransforms` / `drawFrameToPano`) instead of translation only, so panned/rolled footage stitches correctly out of the box. The independent **Allow Frame Scaling** guard (`panoAllowFrameScale`) is left at its `false` default, so the per-frame rigid transform still excludes the noisy scale term — this default change does NOT reintroduce the scale drift that an unguarded full similarity would. Users can still toggle Rotate Frames (SfM) off per session.

---

## Version 2.78.1 (2026-06-07)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed synthetic clouds shedding puffs and rendering non-deterministically as the look camera moves** (`binSort()` in `src/nodes/CNodeSynthClouds.js`). `binSort` re-sorts the cloud's instanced puffs back-to-front (farthest first) for correct alpha blending, and runs whenever the look camera moves past a threshold. The old hand-rolled bucket sort was lossy: it computed `minDist`/`maxDist`, scattered the `n` puffs into `NUM_BINS = n` distance bins via `binCounts`/`binStarts`/`binCurrent`, and could miscount so that a puff was dropped on every call — leaving a `[0,0,0]` hole in `instanceOffsets`. Because the sort fires on camera motion, this made a cloud (a) render slightly differently between page loads / sessions (non-deterministic) and (b) slowly lose puffs as the camera panned. The rewrite sorts an index permutation instead: it builds `order = [0..n)`, does `order.sort((a, b) => distances[b] - distances[a])` (descending distance², i.e. farthest-first), then gathers `offsets`/`sizes` into fresh buffers indexed by `order`. This is guaranteed lossless — every puff lands in exactly one destination slot — and deterministic, since `Array.prototype.sort` is stable so equal-distance puffs keep their build order. Because the result is now fully sorted, `this.combGap` is set to `1` (the per-frame comb sort only has to maintain the order) instead of the old `maxBinCount`.

### Improvements
- **Fast-regression harness now auto-recovers environment flakes** (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`; internal test tooling only — not shipped in the app). After the main concurrent run, each `fail`/`error` sitch is re-run once, serially, in its own fresh `chromium.launchPersistentContext` (new browser process = clean in-process GPU/memory state, no settle/tile-load contention): a real regression fails again and stays a failure (`stableFail`), while a sitch that passes solo is relabelled a recovered flake (`recoveredFromFlake`, counts as a pass) and added to a new `recovered` list in `report.json` and the summary line. This automates the manual "re-run it solo" triage so the gate is trustworthy without masking genuine stable diffs. A new `--no-retry` flag (`CONFIG.noRetry`) disables the retry pass, which is also skipped in `--update` mode. The per-result logging was factored into a shared `logResult()` helper.

---

## Version 2.78.0 (2026-06-06)

### Improvements
- **Feature Pano now registers low-contrast / low-frequency footage** (Motion Analysis → Panorama → Feature Pano Options), via `src/FeaturePanoramaExporter.js` and `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`. New detection-tuning controls and a new registration pipeline let "Export Feature Pano" track soft, blurry, low-contrast content (e.g. masked cloud/IR video) that the old sharp-corner ORB path found nothing on:
  - **Feature Scale** (`menu.panorama.featureScale`, slider 1–8, default 1): `detectFeatures()` downsamples the frame by 1/N with `INTER_AREA` (a low-pass average) *before* ORB detection, turning large soft structures (clouds) into detectable corners and washing out pixel noise; keypoints are rescaled back to full-resolution source coordinates so all downstream geometry stays in source pixels.
  - **Feature Contrast** (`menu.panorama.featureContrast`, slider 1–60, default 20): the ORB FAST corner-contrast threshold; lower picks up fainter features.
  - **Feature Count** (`menu.panorama.featureCount`, slider 500–8000, default 2000): the ORB `nfeatures` cap.
  - **Feature Source** (`menu.panorama.featureSource`, default *ORB Features*): switches the registration source between *ORB Features* (re-detect + match ORB corners each frame) and *Motion Tracklets* (reuse the `MotionAnalyzer`'s optical-flow tracklets — the same points followed across many frames, more consistent on soft content). *Motion Tracklets* runs a motion-analysis pass over the range first via `ensureMotionTrackletsForFeaturePano()`, then `motionTrackletCorrespondences(frame)` hands the exporter inlier vectors (`resultCache…flowData.vectors`) as per-frame correspondences; `runFeaturePano`'s `motionMode` branch composes the single-frame fits across the spanned video frames (handles `frameStep > 1`) with no ORB detection/decode in pass 1.
  - **Optimize Feature Tracking** (`menu.panorama.optimizeFeature`): `optimizeFeatureTracking()` grid-searches detect-scale × FAST-threshold (`OPT_SCALES=[1,2,4,8]` × `OPT_THRESHOLDS=[20,10,5,2]`) over a few frames around the current frame (`OPT_FRAME_SPAN=2`, feature cap pinned to `OPT_FEATURE_COUNT=3000` during the sweep), scores each combo by the number of *coherent multi-frame tracklets* it yields (not raw inlier count, which just rewards a flood of low-threshold noise matches), and writes the best scale/contrast/count back into the sliders via the kept `featureDetectScaleController`/`featureFastThresholdController`/`featureCountController`. Reports progress (`status.optimizingPercent`) and a summary (`status.optimizeResult`).
- **More robust feature registration via multi-frame coherent tracklets** (`src/FeaturePanoramaExporter.js`). The ORB source replaces the old independent frame-to-frame matching (`estimatePairwise`, removed) with a chaining tracker: `matchMutual()` does forward-backward ratio matching (a pair must be each other's ratio-passing nearest neighbour, rejecting one-off asymmetric matches), `makeTracker()` chains those mutual matches into multi-frame tracks (dropping any that die below `MIN_TRACKLET_LEN=3` frames to keep memory bounded), and `trackIsCoherent()` keeps only tracks whose per-step velocity stays near its median (within `TRACKLET_COHERENCE_TOL=3` px, tolerating ≤25% jittery steps) — sensor-noise "tracks" jitter randomly and fail. Surviving tracklets' consecutive hops become the per-pair correspondences (`pairCorrespondences()`), each fitted by the new `fitFromCorrespondences()` (RANSAC homography + rigid fit on inliers, median-translation fallback). The live preview now shows persistent tracked points (`tracker.activePersistent()`).

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed Feature Pano finding no features on low-contrast footage** (root cause, `makeORB()` in `src/FeaturePanoramaExporter.js`). Two compounding bugs: (a) this OpenCV.js (WASM) build can't bind the `ORB::ScoreType` enum, so the multi-arg `new cv.ORB(...)` constructor *throws* ("unbound types … ScoreType") — every attempt to pass a custom FAST threshold silently failed and detection ran at stock settings; the detector is now built single-arg and configured with the `setFastThreshold`/`setEdgeThreshold`/`setPatchSize` setters. (b) The default 31px ORB `edgeThreshold` is a fixed border that, on a heavily downscaled detection image, confined detection to the centre of the frame (re-introducing the masked HUD/object and dropping the edge clouds, which also kept the optimizer pinned at scale 1); `edgeThreshold`/`patchSize` now scale with the detection resolution (~8% of the smallest dimension, clamped 8–31px) so detection still reaches the frame edges at high Feature Scale.

---

## Version 2.77.0 (2026-06-06)

### New Features
- **Scripted Video** (Video → Scripted Video): a new text-script cinematic camera/render system in `src/CScriptedVideo.js`, wired via `addScriptedVideoMenu()` from `index.js`/`initializeOnce()`. A line-based DSL (`view` / `text` / `zoom` / `orbit` / `track` / `fov` / `wait`, with `&` for concurrent moves) drives camera moves, view cuts, and on-screen captions. The menu offers *Script Window…* (in-page editor) and *Script Window (New Window)* (pop-out), a *Render Video (1080P60)* action, and a *Render Quality* subfolder (*Wait For Terrain*, *Terrain Detail*, *Motion Blur*, *Super-sample*). The editor (refined in `3cae120e`) is a transparent textarea over a styled backdrop with a draggable/zoomable/scrollable timeline: the current cursor line is bolded, the hovered control number is boxed, and the scroll-wheel edits durations/distances/degrees/fov/`&`-offsets in place (±2-char hover tolerance, 0.1 vs 1 step, ×10 with Shift, per-field floor so a duration can't vanish, span-driven so digits inside caption text or `lat,lon,alt` coords are untouched). Hovering a control number cross-highlights its timeline segment and vice-versa. The preview renders the active view in a centred 16:9 box matching the 1080p export so captions land where they export (view rects are saved/restored on stop). Camera scripting drives only `mainCamera`, leaving the look view matched to the witness video. The offline render takes exclusive control via a `Globals.scriptedVideoRendering` gate in `renderMain` (so the live loop can't fight over view size and oscillate `camera.aspect`); the moving view's `buildings3DTiles` `errorTarget` is raised and its fade disabled so photorealistic tiles settle instead of perpetually cross-fading; export is a 1080P60 MP4 via `MediabunnyExporter`.
- **De-Fence tool** (Motion Analysis → De-Fence): new `src/DefenceExporter.js` removes a close, fast-moving, largely opaque foreground fence from a panned video and reconstructs the distant scene glimpsed through the moving gaps. `analyzeDefence()` does one low-res decode pass caching grays + gap weights, estimates the dominant FENCE motion (short keyframe baselines) and the slower BACKGROUND motion (longer, gap-weighted baselines), builds a fence-aligned temporal-variance map (static boards = low variance), and computes background-aligned mosaic bounds. *Start De-Fence* (`exportDefence()`) runs a second pass accumulating only background pixels into a fence-free mosaic, combined per-pixel by temporal median (`combineMosaic`, mean fallback) to kill streaks. *Export De-Fence Video* (`exportDefenceVideo()`) renders a three-phase process video — original video, fence dissolving as the background accumulates, then a hold on the reconstructed scene — sharing `analyzeDefence`/`combineMosaic` so the held frame matches the PNG. A *Technique* dropdown selects *Colour + Motion* (default), *Colour only* (lit-gap brightness/green gate), or *Motion only* (fence-aligned variance gate, colour-independent; variance sampled bilinearly so sub-pixel fence motion doesn't smear wood-grain into false variance). Sliders: *Variance Threshold*, *Gap Brightness*, *Background Baseline*. UI lives in `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js` / `src/i18n/en.js`. (Known limitation: the variance/residual-motion classification is unsound when a gap moves with the fence.)

### Improvements
- **Allow Frame Scaling** (Motion Analysis → Panorama → Motion Panorama Options): new option (i18n `menu.panorama.allowFrameScale`, default OFF) gating the per-frame transform used by the motion panorama's *Rotate Frames (SfM)* mode. `frameSimilarity()` in `CMotionAnalysisUI.js` previously always stamped frames with the full recovered similarity, whose noisy SCALE term chained frame-to-frame into visible scale drift (frames growing/shrinking along the panorama). It now defaults to a RIGID transform (rotation about the image centre + centre translation, scale = 1), so frames stay the same size; turning *Allow Frame Scaling* on restores the old full-similarity behaviour (e.g. a genuinely zooming camera).
- **Draggable windows close by dragging off the top** (`cef184f3`): `makeDraggable` gains an opt-in `closeOnDragOffTop` callback — dragging a window's handle up under the menu bar dims it to 0.5 ("release to close") and dismisses it on release instead of stranding it off-screen (off by default, so other draggable views/dialogs are unaffected). New helpers `clampBelowMenuBar(el)` and `menuBarBottomPx()` (`#menuBarBlackBar` `offsetTop + offsetHeight`). The Scripted Video window uses drag-off-top to close and re-clamps below the menu bar on every `showWindow()`; the Notes window uses drag-off-top to hide (not when docked) and a `setVisible` override re-clamps below the bar on show.

### Bug Fixes
- **Stop Analysis now cancels the duplicate-frame scan and gap-fill passes** (`5d8b1b42`): `MotionAnalyzer.buildDuplicateFrameMap()` and `fillBadNonDuplicateMotionGaps()` gained a `shouldCancel` parameter; `CMotionAnalysisUI.js` threads `isPanoJobCancelled` through these and the motion/pano export drivers (`analyzeAllFrames`, `exportMotionPanorama`, `exportPanoVideo`). Previously *Stop Analysis* couldn't interrupt these loops, so cancelling a panorama job mid duplicate-detection or gap-fill ran to completion; they now poll the cancel flag and end cleanly.
- **De-Fence / Feature-Pano video exports now release the encoder on cancel/error** (`b0792025`): `MediabunnyExporter` gained an idempotent `dispose()` (closes the WebCodecs encoder and cancels the muxer) and a `finalized` flag; the De-Fence and Feature-Pano video `finally` blocks now actually free the encoder instead of hitting a dead guard when an export is cancelled or errors before finalize.

---

## Version 2.76.1 (2026-06-06)

### Improvements
- **The Masking folder is now a persistent menu, available as soon as a video is loaded** (Video → Motion Analysis → Masking). Previously the entire *Masking* folder (Enable Mask, Edit Mask, Brush Size, Clear Mask, Auto Mask + its params, Auto Mask Redactions + its params — 19 controls) was built inside `createParamSliders()` in `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`, which is created on *Start Analysis* and destroyed on *Stop Analysis*; masking was therefore unreachable without first starting a full motion-analysis pass. The folder is now built once by a new persistent `createMaskingFolder(parentFolder)` called from `addMotionAnalysisMenu()`, with the folder and every control marked `.perm()` so they survive Start/Stop. Each control reads live through to the current analyzer (or a documented default before one exists) via `Object.defineProperty` getters, and writes via a new `ensureMaskingAnalyzer()` helper that lazily constructs the `MotionAnalyzer` + mask overlay on first interaction — but only if a video is actually loaded (`NodeMan.get("video", false)` with a `videoData` check; returns `null` otherwise). To support standalone masking, `MotionAnalyzer` (`src/CMotionAnalysis.js`) splits a new idempotent `ensureMaskOverlay()` out of `createOverlays()`: it creates ONLY the `CNodeMaskOverlay` (`motionMaskOverlay`), not the speed/graph HUD canvases, so masking works without the motion-vector overlays. `createOverlays()` now calls `ensureMaskOverlay()` and reuses the already-created overlay if standalone masking made it first. If analysis is later started, `startAnalysis()` reuses the same lazily-created analyzer instance.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed "Stop Analysis" not cancelling an in-progress panorama export.** In `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`, clicking *Stop Analysis* during a long export previously only tore down the live analysis overlay (`motionAnalyzer.stop()`); the export's own frame loop kept running to completion in the background. A cooperative cancellation flag now threads through every long-running loop: `toggleMotionAnalysis()` sets `panoCancelRequested = true` when stopping, each export clears it at its start via `beginPanoJob()`, and every loop polls `isPanoJobCancelled()`. Coverage: `analyzeAllFrames()` bails in the observer-wait loop, both retry passes, the final gap-fill, and returns `false` when cancelled; `exportMotionPanorama()` breaks its draw loop, then removes the preview overlay, resets its menu label (`menu.panorama.exportImage.label`), restores `par.frame`/`par.paused`, clears `Globals.justVideoAnalysis`, and returns without saving a PNG; `exportPanoVideo()` breaks both the pano-build loop and the video-encode loop and gates the final save behind `progress.shouldSave() && !isPanoJobCancelled()`, restoring state and resetting its label on cancel. The four export entry points (`exportMotionPanorama`, `exportPanoVideo`, `exportFeaturePano`, `exportFeaturePanoVideo`) all call `beginPanoJob()` first. The feature-pano exporter (`src/FeaturePanoramaExporter.js`) already had its own *Cancel* button and a local `cancelled` flag; it now also accepts an external `shouldCancel` callback (`isCancelled = () => cancelled || externalCancel()`), threaded in from the menu as `shouldCancel: isPanoJobCancelled`, so *Stop Analysis* cancels the feature-detect loop, the stitch loop, and the video render. On cancel no partial output is saved. Verified live on the gofast sitch via MCP: a Motion Pano Image export cancelled mid-analysis ended cleanly with the analyzer stopped, the export aborted, no PNG saved, no leftover overlay, and `par` state restored.

## Version 2.76.0 (2026-06-06)

### New Features
- **Feature-based panorama stitcher: Export Feature Pano Image / Video** (Video → Motion Analysis → Panorama). New `src/FeaturePanoramaExporter.js` registers video frames with OpenCV ORB feature detection + `BFMatcher` knn matching (Lowe ratio test) + `findHomography(RANSAC)`, then warps each frame to fit — handling the camera rotation and perspective distortion that the translation-only *Export Motion Pano* cannot. The exporter **auto-selects the projection model** (i18n `featureProjection`, *Projection* dropdown with `Auto`/`Planar`/`Rigid`): a full-homography **planar mosaic** for translating/aerial captures (preserves perspective), falling back to a drift-proof **rigid** (rotation+translation) mosaic when the planar chain blows up — detected by comparing the planar bounding box against the rigid one. The rigid path produces the correct flat/cylindrical unrolling for a rotating camera and needs no focal length. Blending is feathered-alpha source-over; the redaction mask + crop ring are used as the ORB detection mask so static HUD/graphics overlays don't corrupt registration; per-iteration `cv.Mat` lifecycle is strictly managed. *Export Feature Pano Video* (i18n `exportFeatureVideo`) renders an MP4 with each source frame warped — by the same transform that stitched it — into its place on a dimmed feature-stitched background, with a cyan outline marking the current frame. New *Feature Pano Options* folder (i18n `featureOptions`): *Frame Step* (`featureFrameStep`), *Crop* (`featureCrop`), *Use Mask* (`featureUseMask`), *Projection* (`featureProjection`). New progress strings `detectingFeaturesPercent` ("Detecting & matching features…") and `stitchingPercent` ("Stitching panorama…"); during the detect-and-match stage the live frame is shown with its RANSAC-inlier matched features circled and a running count, refreshing on a ~3s cadence. English i18n only (other locales fall back).

### Improvements
- **Restructured Panorama menu** (`src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`, Video → Motion Analysis → Panorama). The old single *Export Motion Panorama* / *Export Pano Video* pair is reorganized into four export actions plus two options folders: *Export Motion Pano Image*, *Export Motion Pano Video*, and a *Motion Pano Options* folder (i18n `motionOptions`) holding the existing pano settings (Rotate Frames, Frame Step, Crop, Use Mask, Analyze/Export With Effects, Remove Outer Black); *Export Feature Pano Image*, *Export Feature Pano Video*, and a *Feature Pano Options* folder. *Stabilize Video* (i18n `stabilize`) is moved up one level from inside Panorama to the Motion Analysis level. Shared `computePanoLayout` keeps the image and video pano-build paths DRY.
- **Motion Pano "Rotate Frames (SfM)" option** (i18n `rotateFrames`, *Motion Pano Options*, off by default; applies to both Motion Pano Image and Video). When enabled it stamps each frame with a full recovered per-frame similarity (rotation + translation, off-center safe) instead of translation-only placement, so it handles camera roll/rotation coherently like the feature pano rather than producing translation-only distortion. The similarity is recovered by refitting the motion analyzer's cached inlier optical-flow vectors with the shared `fitSimilarity` helper, now exported from `src/CameraMotionFromVideo.js`. It is a new gated pathway — the translation-only path is unchanged when the option is off.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed all-black Pano Video / Panorama exports produced by an over-limit canvas.** `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js` (`calculatePanoDimensions`) and `src/PanoramaExporter.js` (`exportPanorama`) previously capped only WIDTH (20000) and left height and total area uncapped. A tall 2D camera sweep could therefore produce a panorama canvas whose total area exceeded the browser's max 2D-canvas area (~268–320M px). Such a canvas fails silently — `createElement` / setting `width` / `drawImage` all succeed, but the backing store never allocates, so the canvas reads back fully black — and the export then showed only the per-frame overlay (drawn onto the valid 4K output) over a black background instead of the stitched panorama. The width-only cap is replaced with `panoFitScale()`, which uniformly downscales to satisfy BOTH a per-dimension cap (16384) and a total-area cap (~134M px). Verified on the *Long Pano Test* sitch: the old 20000×17379 (347M px) canvas read back `[0,0,0,0]`; the new 12428×10799 (134M px) canvas renders the stitched frames correctly.
- **Fixed the Switch-to-Ground-Track frame being silently reset to 0 on load.** `src/nodes/CNodeCamera.js`: `updateGroundTrackSwitchFrameRange()` destructively clamped the stored `switchToGroundTrackFrame` down to the current max whenever `Sit.frames` changed. During load `Sit.frames` is transiently small (the video decoder reports its final frame count asynchronously), so a saved value such as 173 was permanently clamped to 0 and then serialized as 0 — silently corrupting the setting. The range updater now only refreshes the GUI display range; the stored value (the user's intent) is left intact and is clamped safely at use-time in `getGroundTrackSwitchFrame()`.

## Version 2.75.1 (2026-06-05)

### Improvements
- **Auto Mask Redactions is now colour-agnostic and fills the slivers between adjacent boxes** (Video → Motion Analysis → Masking). `src/RedactionDetect.js` was reworked. The saturation / "greyness" signal (`colorSpread`) is removed entirely, so a redaction box of *any* colour (black, grey, white-ish, thermal palette, …) is now caught; the only remaining colour-ish test is the `maxLuma` brightness ceiling (a guard against flat, invariant bright sky). A pixel "seed" is now just *not-too-bright + spatially flat + temporally invariant*. Detection became a **two-pass** design to safely close the thin, non-flat *transition seams* between adjacent boxes (e.g. a grey sidebar next to a black box) without bridging into terrain — necessary because in a near-static camera window a large fraction of the terrain is also temporally invariant, so a naïve global close would merge a box into an invariant-terrain blob, break its rectangularity, and get the whole box rejected and deleted. **Pass 1** (`_qualifyComponents`) qualifies the confident redaction *masses*: connected components whose width AND height ≥ `minSize`, not essentially the whole frame, whose axis-aligned rectangle decomposition covers ≥ `fill` of the component — this is the step that still excludes textured terrain. **Pass 2** bridges the non-flat seams *only* between qualified masses by dilating the qualified mask gated to seed/qualified pixels (growth can never enter moving footage), dilating again across the seam, then eroding back; because terrain was removed in Pass 1, bridging can never connect a box to terrain. The bridged masses are decomposed into rectangles, and a new rectangle edge-snap (`_snapRects`) snaps nearby parallel overlapping rect edges together to close any residual rect-level sliver, before the existing per-rect `spread` expand and frame-edge snap. New `snap` option (default 6) caps the sliver width bridged. `MotionAnalyzer` (`src/CMotionAnalysis.js`) drops `redactionColorSpread`, adds `redactionSnap = 6`, and changes `redactionSpread` default 3 → 8; `autoMaskRedactions()` passes `snap` instead of `colorSpread` to `detectRedactionRects`. Net user-facing effect: the grey strips/seams between black-and-grey redaction boxes are now masked solidly instead of leaving unmasked slivers that leaked into panoramas, and boxes of any colour are detected. Verified by two new cases in `tests/RedactionDetect.test.js` (a coloured non-grey flat invariant box is detected; seam-bridging between two stacked boxes via `snap`).
- **Masking menu sliders updated to match the new detector** (`src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`, Video → Motion Analysis → Masking). The *Redaction Greyness* slider (`redactionColorSpread`) is removed (the detector no longer tests colour). A new *Redaction Snap* slider is added (`redactionSnap`, range 0–30, default 6, tooltip "Bridge slivers up to this many px between adjacent boxes (closes grey↔black transition gaps; 0 = off)"). *Redaction Expand* (`redactionSpread`) widens from 0–10 to 0–20 and its default goes 3 → 8. `serializeMotionAnalysis` / `deserializeMotionAnalysis` and the slider-init defaults are updated accordingly (`colorSpread` dropped, `snap` added), so settings still round-trip with the sitch.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed exported motion panoramas showing dark vertical "edge-dropping" lines, especially with Panorama Crop turned up.** `src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`: "Panorama Crop" (`panoCrop`, Video → Motion Analysis → Panorama) is reimplemented as **masking** instead of cutting source geometry. Previously `calculatePanoDimensions` shrank each frame by `2*crop` and `drawFrameToPano` drew a `crop`-inset source rectangle; that hard source-rect edge caused the canvas `drawImage` to bilinear-sample the dark cropped-away border, producing dark seams that worsened as crop increased, and the inset also introduced a hidden −`crop` misregistration. Now `calculatePanoDimensions` keeps the full frame size (`croppedWidth/Height = frameWidth/Height`, panorama dimensions no longer change with crop), and `drawFrameToPano` builds a temp frame whose outer `crop`-px ring is set transparent (alpha 0) alongside any redaction-mask pixels, then draws the **full** frame at its true motion offset — the ignored border is simply filled by neighbouring frames. `tempCanvas` is now allocated whenever `useMask || crop > 0` (in both `exportMotionPanorama` and `exportPanoVideo`), and the pano-video frame overlay draws the full frame (`drawImage` source rect changed from `crop, crop, …` to `0, 0, …`). (An `imageSmoothingEnabled = false` line was tried and reverted; it is not in the final code.)
- **Fixed the mask overlay not tracking the video's pan and zoom.** `src/nodes/CNodeMaskOverlay.js`: `renderCanvas` previously drew the mask canvas with a fixed dest rect, so when the video view was panned/zoomed the mask stayed put while the footage moved underneath it (even though mouse painting was already pan/zoom-aware via `canvasToVideoCoords`). The mask is now drawn through the *same* source-crop transform the video frame uses — `ctx.drawImage(maskCanvas, ov.sx, ov.sy, ov.sWidth, ov.sHeight, ov.dx, ov.dy, ov.dWidth, ov.dHeight)` — so it registers exactly with the frame and the Point Track (with a legacy `zoomView` fallback for the `posLeft/posTop`-driven path). The brush cursor radius now divides by `sWidth` instead of `videoWidth` (`sWidth` shrinks with zoom; equal to `videoWidth` when unzoomed), so the painted-area indicator stays the correct size as you zoom.

## Version 2.75.0 (2026-06-05)

### New Features
- **Auto Mask Redactions** (Video → Motion Analysis → Masking → *Auto Mask Redactions*) — a new masking tool that detects solid black/grey rectangular redaction boxes painted over footage and adds them to the motion-analysis mask, so those boxes are excluded from optical-flow / motion estimation. Detection lives in a new dependency-free `src/RedactionDetect.js` (`detectRedactionRects(frames, width, height, opts)`, no OpenCV/DOM/Three imports, unit-tested in `tests/RedactionDetect.test.js`). A pixel is a redaction candidate only when it passes four signals: (1) low saturation / "grey" (RGB channel spread ≤ `colorSpread`) and not too bright (luma ≤ `maxLuma`, i.e. black..mid-grey); (2) spatially **flat** — its luma barely differs from neighbours at radius 2 (≤ `flatness`), the key discriminator against textured terrain that momentarily holds still under a slow/static camera; (3) temporally **invariant** — luma range across a decoded frame window stays within `invariance` % (the box covers the moving scene, so it does not change); (4) **rectilinear** — after a morphological close→open, connected components are decomposed by `_componentRects` into axis-aligned rectangles that must cover ≥ `fill` of the component (covers overlapping L/T boxes and a grey sidebar with embedded black boxes precisely, while real-terrain blobs decompose into sub-`minSize` slivers and are rejected). Surviving rectangles are expanded by `spread` px and edge-snapped to the frame border (within `edgeSnap` = 5% of frame size) to avoid leaving thin unmasked slivers. `MotionAnalyzer.autoMaskRedactions()` (`src/CMotionAnalysis.js`) loads a centred, clamped window of *actively decoded* frames via `_loadMaskFrames(centerFrame, windowSize)` (awaits `waitForFrame` so invariance has real evidence, never collapses to one frame at the clip ends), runs detection, and paints the rects into the mask; a monotonic `_redactionRunId` guard means a slow earlier run (e.g. mid slider-drag) can never overwrite a newer one. Tuning sliders added to the Masking menu (`src/CMotionAnalysisUI.js`), all serialized round-trip: *Redaction Frames* (`redactionWindow`, default 8), *Redaction Invariance %* (`redactionInvariance`, 5), *Redaction Max Bright* (`redactionMaxLuma`, 180), *Redaction Greyness* (`redactionColorSpread`, 24 — no effect on monochrome/thermal video), *Redaction Flatness* (`redactionFlatness`, 10 — "this is what rejects textured terrain"), *Redaction Min Size* (`redactionMinSize`, 12), *Redaction Min Fill* (`redactionFill`, 0.6), *Redaction Expand* (`redactionSpread`, 3). Each slider's `onChange` re-runs the tool live.

### Improvements
- **Auto-mask tools are now additive instead of mask-clearing.** Previously the text *Auto Mask* tool called `maskCtx.clearRect(...)` over the whole mask before painting, wiping hand-painting and any other auto output. Now both *Auto Mask* (`MotionAnalyzer.autoMask`) and the new *Auto Mask Redactions* (`autoMaskRedactions`) **add** to the existing mask through a shared per-layer helper `MotionAnalyzer._applyAutoMaskLayer(name, drawFn)` (layers `'text'` and `'redaction'`). The helper keeps a per-layer baseline = the mask as it was just before that layer last drew: if the mask is still exactly as the layer last left it (revision match), it restores that baseline and redraws — so re-running a tool (e.g. while dragging a tuning slider) replaces only **its own** contribution idempotently, without stacking; otherwise it snapshots the current mask as the new baseline and draws on top, genuinely adding to whatever paint / clear / other-tool output is there now. The revision match is driven by a new `maskRevision` counter bumped in `CNodeMaskOverlay.saveMask()` (`src/nodes/CNodeMaskOverlay.js`) on every mask mutation. *Clear Mask* still resets everything and now also drops the auto-mask baselines (`_autoMaskBaselines = {}`) so a later auto run starts from the empty mask rather than restoring stale content.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed an intermittent `CVideoImageData: img is undefined` assert when loading an image-as-video under load contention.** `src/nodes/CNodeVideoView.js`: the image-as-video load path trusted `loadAsset().parsed` (the FileManager entry's `.data`) to be a decoded image, but under load contention `loadAsset` can resolve from a list entry whose `.data` isn't populated yet, handing `makeImageVideo` an `undefined` image and tripping `CVideoImageData`'s assert (the intermittent "Atremis Starlink" load failure). Two new helpers fix it: `isDecodedImage(img)` confirms a usable already-decoded drawable (object with `width>0`/`height>0` — rejects an `ArrayBuffer`, null, or a width-0 not-yet-loaded `Image`), and `decodeImageFromBytes(bytes, fileName)` decodes the original bytes into a guaranteed-`onload`-ready `HTMLImageElement` (mirroring the `loadVideoFromEntry` restore path, MIME inferred from extension). The loader now uses `result.parsed` only when `isDecodedImage` passes; otherwise it decodes from `fileEntry.original`, and if even that yields nothing it fails gracefully (logs, decrements `Globals.pendingActions`, clears `videoLoadPending`, returns) instead of asserting. (The bulk of this commit — `tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs` settle-gate determinism: unconditional minWait floor, hard-error on never-reaching the target frame, terrain high-res-upgrade wait, sitch-enumeration fetch retries — is test-harness only and has no user-facing effect.)
- **Fixed a 3D object intermittently rendering the wrong model after an out-of-order async model-load race.** `src/nodes/CNode3DObject.js`: when a `CNode3DObject`'s model changes, `this.currentModel` is set synchronously to the *latest* requested model, then `loadModelAsset(model.file, …)` loads the GLB asynchronously. Because `loadModelAsset` re-parses every time, those loads can complete out of order (a more complex model requested first can finish *after* a simpler model requested later — e.g. the constructor's default-model load completing after `modDeserialize`'s saved-model load, or rapid menu switches). The completion callback assigned `this.model = modelAsset.scene` on a last-writer-wins basis, so a stale earlier load could overwrite the correct/latest model and desync `this.model` from `this.currentModel` — the user saw the wrong model (e.g. the default F/A-18F instead of the saved/selected one), most often right after loading a saved sitch or after switching models quickly. Fix: the load callback now captures the `model` it was started for and bails if it is no longer current — `if (model !== this.currentModel) { Globals.pendingActions--; return; }` — discarding the stale result (with a `console.warn`) while keeping the `Globals.pendingActions` counter balanced. This is the model-load analogue of the video selection race.
  - Same change also adds an assert-safety guard in the old-model cleanup branch: `FileManager.exists(this.currentModel.file) &&` now precedes `FileManager.isUnhosted(this.currentModel.file)`, so rapidly switching away from a still-loading, not-yet-registered model no longer asserts on a missing file inside `isUnhosted`.
- **Fixed a multi-video/multi-image restore intermittently selecting (and mis-pairing) the wrong source after an out-of-order async load.** `src/nodes/CNodeVideoView.js`: restoring a saved sitch with several videos/images drove a sequential restore through `continueVideoRestore`, which paired each async load *completion* with a *positional* slot (`videos[loadedCount]` plus a running `skippedCount`). Because images load via mixed paths — a fast in-memory `Image` path vs. a `newVideo` URL fallback — and can share S3 URLs, completions arrived out of order or with an extra, so a completion-order slot could (a) pair one video's saved label/`staticURL` with a *different* video's decoded `videoData` (a slot swap), and (b) let a late straggler push a duplicate `videos[]` entry that then hijacked the selected index. User-visible symptom: a sitch saved with multiple images/videos could load with the wrong image/video selected — non-deterministic, roughly 1 in 3 loads — e.g. the "Atremis Starlink" sitch intermittently showed the wrong Earth image. Fix makes the restore **identity-based** instead of completion-order-based:
  - `_preCreateRestoreSlots()` pre-creates one `videos[]` output slot per saved entry up front (in saved order, each with `videoData: null`), so every saved entry owns a fixed slot before any load completes, and the saved `currentVideoIndex` (`pr.targetIndex`) is applied immediately. Called from the first `loadVideoFromEntry` of a restore (guarded by `pr._slotsCreated`).
  - Every restore-originated load now carries the source index of the slot it fills (`restoreIndex`), threaded through `newVideo` / `loadVideoFromEntry` / `makeImageVideo` and stamped onto the created `videoData` as `_restoreIndex` (also copied onto the `CVideoPatchedData` wrapper in `wrapWithPatchedIfNeeded` so a wrapped completion still routes correctly).
  - `continueVideoRestore` now reads `loadedVideoData._restoreIndex` and writes the result *only* to its own slot (and only if that slot is still empty), dropping stale/duplicate completions. It advances/starts the next load only when the slot it is currently waiting for (`pr.loadingIndex`) is resolved, so a straggler for a different slot can't advance the chain or kick off a duplicate load.
  - The add-on-load fast paths (`newVideo`, `makeImageVideo`) only call `addVideoEntry` when `restoreIndex === undefined && !this.pendingVideoRestore`, so a restore-originated straggler can never push a duplicate entry — even if it completes after restore has finished and `pendingVideoRestore` is gone.
  - `skipCurrentVideoRestore` marks the in-flight slot `_restoreFailed` (instead of bumping a `skippedCount`) and advances the same index-based way; `_finalizeVideoRestore` compacts out failed slots, shifts the saved target left by any removed-before-target slot, and re-applies the selection authoritatively (`currentVideoIndex = -1` then `selectVideo(finalTarget)`) so the saved selection — never a late add — wins. Auto-tracking re-apply (`deserializeAutoTracking`) moves into `_finalizeVideoRestore` unchanged. This is the video-selection analogue of the 3D-model load race fixed above.

## Version 2.74.2 (2026-06-04)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed sustained ~600% CPU on a loaded, paused sitch (broken render-on-demand).** Render-on-demand could never go back to sleep, so heavy scenes (Google photorealistic 3D tiles + terrain + night sky) full-rendered every tick forever even while paused. The core bug was in `setRenderOne()` (`src/Globals.js`): its truthy-coalescing guard `if (!par.renderOne)` (which exists to avoid redundant wake-ups) silently neutralised `setRenderOne(false)`, so the clear never applied and `par.renderOne` stayed `true` permanently — `renderMain` never took its paused early-out. Fixes span the whole render path:
  - `src/Globals.js`: `setRenderOne(false)` now writes `par.renderOne = false` and returns immediately (a clear always applies and never wakes the loop); the truthy path keeps its coalescing guard.
  - `src/indexRender.js`: `renderMain` clears the flag with a direct `par.renderOne = false` (unambiguous), and the `Globals.fixedFrame` (URL-pinned-frame) block only re-arms `par.frame`/`setRenderOne` when `par.frame !== Globals.fixedFrame`; previously it re-set `par.frame` every tick, which re-armed `renderOne` via the `par.frame` setter and defeated the paused early-out.
  - `src/threeExt.js`: `DebugArrow` only re-applies a layer mask when it actually changed (`DebugArrows[name].layers.mask !== layerMask`), and `propagateLayerMaskObject` / `setLayerMaskRecursive` only call `setRenderOne(true)` when a child mask actually changed. These run from per-frame redraw paths (camera frustum rebuild, flow indicator), so the old unconditional `setRenderOne(true)` re-armed the loop every frame.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeCompassUI.js`: `lastElevation` is now updated unconditionally in `renderCanvas`. Previously it was only stored when elevation was displayed, so the change-guard kept failing every frame (stuck at its init value), redrawing the compass and calling `removeText()`→`setRenderOne(true)` every frame.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeBackgroundFlowIndicator.js`: the two expensive near-horizontal terrain raycasts are now cached and skipped when frame + both camera positions + both LOS directions are unchanged (the arrows are persistent named debug objects, so they stay drawn). The cache is invalidated in `remove()` so a later `show()` recomputes.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeFloodSim.js`: `setRenderOne(true)` now fires only when a flood physics step actually runs (the throttled ~20/s path), not unconditionally every 16 ms.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeContrail.js`: `update()` only requests a render when `frame !== this._lastContrailFrame` (the ribbon rebuild is frame-deterministic; GUI edits already request a render via their onChange handlers).
  - `src/nodes/CNodeBuildings3DTiles.js`: `PerViewTiles.update` skips `TilesRenderer.update()` (which re-traverses the whole tileset and churned ~119 KB/frame across two views on a Google-photorealistic scene) when the camera is static (a per-view `_lastCamFingerprint` over the world matrix + fov/zoom/aspect) and the tileset is fully settled (`_isUpdatePending()` checks queued/downloading/parsing/fading), with a ~1s grace window after the camera stops. A new `tile-visibility-change` listener calls `setRenderOne(true)` so late-arriving / LOD-swapped / fading tiles get drawn, and the node self-disables `updateWhilePaused` once settled so the render loop can actually sleep.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeTerrainUI.js`: the subdivision keep-alive grace is now gated on `pendingTileLoads.size` (real in-flight network/decode) instead of `_dirtyParents.size`, which created a feedback loop (a coverage pass flips a tile's active state as a side effect, refreshing the grace, running another pass forever — churning at ~600% CPU on a static camera). The node also self-disables `updateWhilePaused` once the terrain is fully settled (no grace, nothing loading via `pendingTileLoads` or per-tile `isLoadingElevation`, no pending recalc/refresh), so the loop sleeps; a camera move or async tile-load completion wakes it.
  - `src/nodes/CNodeTerrain.js`: the debounced `elevationChanged` dispatch now calls `setRenderOne(true)` after late elevation tiles mutate the mesh, so freshly-arrived elevation is drawn instead of showing stale flat/coarse terrain until the user nudges the camera. This same render-on-demand fix also corrects the camera frustum occasionally rendering at the wrong FOV in video sitches that match the video aspect (the frustum rebuild's per-frame layer-mask reapply was part of the self-sustaining loop, and the static-camera early-outs now fold camera fov/aspect into the settle fingerprint).
- **Fixed stuttering and ~1-second hangs when playing high-resolution (e.g. 4K) video.** `src/CVideoDecodeWorker.js`: the WebCodecs decode worker decoded a whole group of chunks (80–90) in one synchronous loop with no backpressure, flooding the pipeline with GBs of external memory in flight (each 4K `VideoFrame`/`ImageBitmap` is ~33 MB), which made V8 fire back-to-back Major GCs that reclaimed almost nothing — the worker spent its time collecting instead of decoding. Added decode backpressure: the decode loop awaits a drain whenever `decoder.decodeQueueSize + inFlightFrames` exceeds `MAX_IN_FLIGHT_FRAMES` (12, a ~400 MB ceiling at 4K), with `inFlightFrames` decremented in the bitmap-post `.finally()` (`frameDrained`). A `decodeGeneration` counter aborts and wakes a suspended decode loop on decoder error (`recoverDecoder`), `reset`, `configure`, and `dispose`, so a fatal/corrupt-stream case can't wedge the loop until the main-thread 45s group timeout.
- **Fixed a Three.js console warning on synthetic buildings.** `src/nodes/CNodeSynthBuilding.js` no longer passes `flatShading: true` to a `MeshBasicMaterial` (which is unlit and warns about the unsupported property); `flatShading` is now only applied for non-`basic` (lit) materials.

### Improvements
- **Quality performance preset no longer decodes full-resolution video by default.** `src/CustomSupport.js`: the Quality preset's `videoMaxSize` changed from `"None"` (full source) to `"1080P"`, so even the highest preset caps decode resolution at 1080P and no preset decodes full 4K by default (complementing the decode-worker backpressure fix above). The other presets are unchanged (Balanced 720P, Fast 480P, Potato 360P).

## Version 2.74.1 (2026-06-03)

### Bug Fixes
- **Deterministic auto-assigned track colours** (`src/TrackManager.js`): auto-palette track colours were indexed by `TrackManager.size()` at track *creation* time. Imported tracks are created in async load-completion order (each file calls `addTracks` as it finishes parsing), so the palette index per track varied run-to-run — the whole palette rotated and every track was recoloured. This caused run-to-run track-colour flicker for users loading the same multi-track sitch, and intermittent visual-regression failures on multi-track sitches (e.g. the Potomac collision sitch). Fix: the palette is extracted to a module const `TRACK_PALETTE` (yellow deliberately skipped — it's the traverse colour), and a new `reassignTrackColors()` runs at the end of `addTracks`, *before* `NodeMan.recalculateAllRootFirst()`, so the colour constants are stable before the line geometry rebuilds. It collects every track flagged `paletteColored` with a `shortName`, sorts them by `shortName` (order-independent → deterministic and distinct), and assigns `TRACK_PALETTE[rank % length]`, updating the per-track colour `CNodeConstant`s (`colorData_<short>` / `colorTrack_<short>`) and the display nodes' `dropColor` (track colour × 0.75 on `trackDisplayDataNode` / `trackDisplayNode`). Only auto-palette tracks are touched: sonde (radiosonde) tracks stay white and aren't marked `paletteColored`, and user/serialised colours are left alone. The provisional `size()`-indexed colour is still set at creation but is now always overridden by the deterministic pass (tracks also now carry `paletteColored` and `shortName`).

### Internal / Refactor
- **Fast-regression harness defaults to headless** (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`, `README.md`, test-only): the harness now launches Chrome headless by default — `headless: !flag('headed')`. New-headless Chrome uses the *same* ANGLE Metal GPU backend as headed (verified `ANGLE Metal Renderer` in both) at the same speed and shares the same persistent profile/cache, but draws no on-screen window, so repeated local runs no longer steal keyboard focus while you work. A new `--headed` flag opts back into a visible window (e.g. to watch a single sitch); `--headless` is kept as an accepted no-op. NOTE: headed and headless ANGLE rasterize ~0.1–0.5% differently, so baselines are mode-specific — regenerate the (local-only) baselines if you switch modes. `README.md` flag table updated to match.

## Version 2.74.0 (2026-06-03)

### New Features
- **Tear-out menu sub-folders (floating / sidebar-docked sub-menus)** — the menu-bar drag system (`CGuiMenuBar` in `src/lil-gui-extras.js`) now lets any *folder* (sub-menu) inside a menu-bar menu be dragged out into its own floating window, in addition to the existing tear-out of whole top-level menus.
  - **Interaction:** `wireFolderTearout(folder)` (idempotent, guarded by `folder._tearoutWired`) wires each folder's `$title`. Dragging the title past a small movement threshold tears the folder out at the cursor into a floating container; a plain click below threshold still just expands/collapses the folder (the gesture temporarily locks `lockOpenClose` to disambiguate). Dropping on a screen edge or the center divider docks the folder into the left/right/center sidebar, reusing the same drop indicators as top-level menus. A sidebar-docked folder pops back into a cursor-following floating window on first drag (`removeMenuFromLeftSidebar`/`Right`/`Center`).
  - **Restore (= "close"):** double-click the title (`dblclick` on `$title`, plus an Android double-tap fallback mirroring the top-level menu handling), drag off the top of the screen, or drop it mostly off-screen (`isMenuOffScreen`). All paths call `restoreFolderToParent(folder)`.
  - **Design:** only the folder's `domElement` is relocated into `folder._detachedContainer`; the GUI object stays in its parent's `children`/`folders` arrays, so `gui.root`, `.listen()`, path-based serialization, nested tear-out and `destroy()` cascades keep working. A comment-node placeholder (`document.createComment("torn-out-submenu")`, stored as `folder._detachPlaceholder`) marks the exact DOM slot to restore into without defeating lil-gui's `.children:empty` styling. The menu bar tracks torn-out folders in a `detachedFolders` Set and a `folder.mode` of `"DOCKED"` / `"DRAGGING"` / `"DETACHED"`. `_isFolderDetached()` excludes torn-out folders from a parent menu's content accounting.
  - **Serialization:** each folder's saved state gains an optional `detached:{mode, left, top, zIndex, sidebarIndex?}`. `modDeserialize` re-tears-out folders (sidebar order merged with that of top-level menus via `getLeftSidebarMenuIndex`/etc.) and `_restoreAllDetachedFolders()` restores every torn-out folder first on reset/destroy/deserialize so no floating/sidebar containers leak across sitch loads. Round-trips with multiple floating and sidebar-docked sub-menus, including nested ones.
  - **Re-entrancy guard:** sidebar show/hide dispatches a synchronous window `resize`. Dragged folders use `mode === "DRAGGING"` and batch operations bump a depth counter so the off-screen auto-restore check (`_checkFloatingMenusOnResize`) only acts on settled `"DETACHED"` folders and can't re-enter on a half-placed folder. (Commit `9478956b`.)

### Internal / Refactor
- **LLA→ECEF conversion cleanup** (`src/LLA-ECEF-ENU.ts`, no user-facing change): removed vestigial machinery that derived the EUS origin and ENU rotation matrix from `Sit.lat`/`Sit.lon` — dead since the ECEF transition, where `LLAToECEFRadians` returns a raw ECEF position with no origin subtraction or rotation. The private `_updateSitConstants` is renamed `_updateEarthModelConstants` and now computes only the two ellipsoid constants (`_llaecef_e2`, `_llaecef_ratio`) that depend solely on the earth-model radii; its change-detection cache key drops from `(Sit.lat, Sit.lon, equatorRadius, polarRadius)` to `(equatorRadius, polarRadius)`. The dead pre-computed origin (`_originEcef*`) and rotation-matrix (`_m00`…`_m22`) globals and the `Sit` import are gone. The long-deprecated, always-ignored optional `radius` parameter (and its `assert`s) is removed from `LLAToECEFRadians` / `LLAToECEF` / `LLAVToECEF` — no caller anywhere passed it. The now-dead exported `updateLLAToECEFConstants()` (zero callers) is deleted. Verified numerically bit-for-bit identical ECEF output (2205-sample test across multiple earth models) and visually via the full fast-regression suite (0 scene diffs).
- **Fast-regression harness: current-worktree base URL** (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`, test-only): the harness no longer defaults its base URL to a now-removed `/regress/` deployment. `currentWorktreeBase()` derives the base from the current git worktree's build folder via `config/config-install.js`'s `buildFolder` (`main` → `local.metabunk.org/sitrec/`, any other branch → `local.metabunk.org/<branch>/`), with a git-branch fallback when the gitignored config is absent (e.g. fresh clone). `--base=` / `SITREC_REGRESS_BASE` still override. `README.md` updated to match.
- **Fast-regression harness: per-sitch frame overrides** (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`, test-only): adds a `FRAME_OVERRIDES` map and `frameFor(sitch)` helper so individual sitches can be captured at a sitch-specific frame instead of the global `CONFIG.frame` (10). Default entry `'Mosul Orb': 62` (the orb is only in a representative position by frame 62, as the old Playwright suite captured it); a `--frame-overrides="Name=62,Other=120"` CLI option merges in more. Wired into `buildLoadUrl` and the frame-mismatch note.
- **Trim legacy Playwright visual-regression registry** (`test-registry.js`, `tests_regression/regression.test.js`, test-only): removes the `testquick`, `ocean`, `gimbal`, `starlink`, `orion`, and `mosul` entries, now covered by the fast-regression harness (dropping `gimbal` also follows the project's no-gimbal-testing convention).

## Version 2.73.1 (2026-06-03)

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed Video Info Display labels drifting and growing when the video is zoomed or panned.** The optional on-video readouts (filename, frame number, timecode, timestamp, dates, etc., drawn by `CNodeVideoInfoUI`) had two problems under zoom/pan: (a) each label's font was scaled by the live, zoom-inflated destination height (`rect.h`), so text grew as you zoomed in; and (b) labels were pinned to the screen rather than to the video content, so they slid off the point they annotated when the video was panned. The fix treats each stored label position (`timecodeX/Y` etc.) as a fraction (0–100) of the *video frame* rather than the canvas, and routes reads/writes through the video view's existing source→dest transform via two new helpers, `pctToCanvas` / `canvasToPct` (which call `CNodeVideoView.videoToCanvasCoords` / `canvasToVideoCoords`, covering both the source-crop zoom and the pos-based mouse zoom). `drawInfoToContext` now takes a `videoView` argument and, when one is present (`live`), maps positions through a `mapPos` closure and sizes the font/padding off `baseHeight` — the unzoomed fit-to-view height computed from the source-vs-view aspect ratio — instead of `rect.h`, so labels keep a constant on-screen size at any zoom. The drag handlers (`dragOffsetX/Y` setup and the drag-move position write) were updated to use the same `pctToCanvas` / `canvasToPct` conversion. The stabilized-video exporter passes no `videoView`, so it keeps the prior native-resolution percent-of-frame layout (`widthPx`/`heightPx`) and is unchanged.

### Internal
- The fast-regression harness (`tests_regression/fast-regression/run.mjs`) now enumerates "Regression"-labelled sitches across multiple test-user accounts via a new `--user=1,99999999` option (default users 1 and 99999999), taking the union of labelled sitch names and resolving the owning user per sitch. Test-harness only; no user-facing effect.

## Version 2.73.0 (2026-06-03)

### New Features
- **Custom Graph** (Show → Graphs → *Add Custom Graph*): a new fully-configurable graph window. `CCustomGraphManager` adds the *Add Custom Graph* button and, per graph, a subfolder of controls (`graphControls` i18n keys: Title / Show / Dark / Toggle Legend / X / Y1 (left) / Y2 (right) / Y3 (right) / Remove) that serialize round-trip. `CNodeCustomGraphView` (extends `CNodeOSDGraphView`) renders Y1 on the left axis and Y2/Y3 on the right, with dark/light theme, legend toggle, on-canvas title, per-series fixed ranges, an empty-state message, drag-to-scrub of the frame line, and a Frame A→B range option. Data comes from a new runtime registry, `CGraphDataManager` (pull-only, version-polled), populated with Camera Motion, Point Track, Analyze Motion (raw + smoothed), Horizon, all OSD series, and per-track heading/speed. Wired into `CustomManager` setup/serialize with `dispose` teardown.
- **Sun-vs-LOS angle series** (Custom Graph data sources): for each `CNodeDisplayLOS` node, `CCustomGraphManager` registers three series under a new *Sun* group — `sunLOS.<id>.angle` = acos(F·S), the true angle between the look camera's per-frame line-of-sight forward F and the direction to the Sun S; `sunLOS.<id>.up` = signed elevation offset (atan2 in the LOS vertical plane); `sunLOS.<id>.left` = signed azimuth offset. LOS-display ids vary per sitch and may be plural, so they are enumerated like tracks/OSD. The up/left basis is roll-independent (built from world-up at the camera); the Sun direction is cached per frame and cleared on dispose.
- **Y-compress** (View menu): a slider (range 1–20, default 1) on the mainView that anamorphically squashes the vertical view frustum. `CNodeView3D` divides `projectionMatrix.elements[5]` (the vertical NDC scale, 1/tan(fovV/2)) by the factor so straight lines stay straight; it is applied both in the render pass (patched onto `updateProjectionMatrix`, composed over the video-pan patch and restored LIFO so it survives `matchVideoAspect` re-apply after `renderSky`) and in `prepareCameraForLOD()` so terrain tile culling sees the widened frustum and loads the newly-exposed tiles (without this the new vertical band showed black gaps). A top-left "Y-compress=N.Nx" DOM overlay (`updateYCompressIndicator()`) appears when active and is restored on sitch load (`addSimpleSerial("yCompress")` + `modDeserialize`).
- **SHF Starlink-flare predictor — real satellites by default** (`tools/shf/`): the standalone flare-prediction tool now downloads the current Starlink TLE in the background at startup and uses it whenever fresh (< 1 day old), falling back to a synthetic constellation only when offline/unavailable or when the requested date is outside the TLE's useful range (the synthetic set is anchored to that date so the seasonal geometry stays meaningful). When real data is in range but unavailable, the in-results synthetic note becomes a *CLICK HERE TO LOAD CURRENT SATELLITES* button. The live scanning view now uses the same 3-line header (Flares / Look / place) and compass/wide-view layout as the final screen so nothing jumps on completion, renders the wide view in replay mode with a dim streak backdrop, and fires a one-shot dot burst per newly-found flare (a "flurry"). "Flares visible" is renamed *Flares* and dead space between sections is tightened.

### Improvements
- **Camera-motion smoothing supports even windows** (Camera Motion): `smoothMotion` (`CameraMotionFromVideo.js`) now accepts even smoothing windows via integer offsets, which cancel a 2-frame source cadence (interlacing/pulldown) outright; the default window changes from 3 to 4. The Smoothing-window slider now allows even values and re-smooths the cached raw motion live (`setMotion` + `recalculateCascade`) with no video re-read.
- **Faster "Open in Sitrec" handoffs on hours-long sitches.** App handoffs build 88k–432k-frame sitches; dragging the camera loaded new elevation tiles and each tile re-cascaded the inherited LOS-analysis graph, baking O(frames) arrays. `CNodeTerrain` now coalesces per-tile `elevationChanged` into a single `scheduleElevationChanged` dispatch per frame (N tiles → 1 cascade). `fromApp` generalizes the existing lightweight gating (which hid unused target/traverse/measurement leaves, formerly `appFlight`-only) to the fixed-observer scene via a new persisted `Sit.appFromApp` marker, and hides the camera display track in fixed mode (a fixed observer's "track" is a single point). `CNodeDisplayTrack` caps sampled line points at 4000 so `recalculate()` stays bounded on very long sitches (normal-length tracks unaffected). Full recalc 276 ms → 146 ms at 88k frames.
