## Version 2.63.0 (2026-05-28)

### New Features
- **Annotate overlay on video** (Video → Annotate): new `CNodeAnnotateOverlay` provides a drawing surface composited on top of the video view, auto-created on any custom sitch with a video. *Show Annotations* is the master switch; *Edit Mode* reveals a toolbar (select, pencil, brush, line, arrow, rectangle, ellipse, text, image drop, eraser — hotkeys S/P/B/L/A/R/E/T/I/X), captures the mouse, and suppresses video pan via `_isAnnotateEditing()`. Selection supports corner handles for rect/ellipse/image, endpoint handles for line/arrow, and live color/width edits. Strokes are stored in original-video pixel coordinates so they follow zoom, pan, resize, and reload (`modSerialize`/`modDeserialize`). *Fade Frames* makes strokes vanish N frames after creation (0 = persistent). Exports respect annotations: single-frame PNG/JPG composites the overlay canvas, *Render Source Video* (in `VideoExporter`) composites strokes onto the source canvas at original-video resolution honoring zoom/pan, and *Render Viewport Video* picks them up via the normal view iteration. Created from `CustomManagerSetup.js` rather than `SitCustom.js` so older saves get the overlay on reload.
- **Lighting Preset dropdown** (Lighting menu): a *Lighting Preset* dropdown sits above the six light sliders in `CNodeLighting`. *Default* snapshots each sitch's construction-time values for those sliders, the master shadows toggle, and the lookView atmosphere flag. *Shadows* applies a fixed set (0.07, 0.8, 1.02, 0, 1, 0.22) with shadows on and atmosphere off, tuned for shadow rendering. The chosen preset label is serialized so a saved sitch remembers the user's last pick. Orbit Image Set (`ExportImageSet.js`) gained a mirror of the same dropdown, attached lazily via `folder.onOpenClose` because `Sit.setup()` (which builds the lighting node) runs after `FileManager.sitchChanged()` on initial load.
- **Atmosphere relocated to Lighting** (Lighting → Atmosphere Tweaks): atmosphere controls move out of the Effects menu into a new *Atmosphere Tweaks* subfolder under Lighting, with the master *Atmosphere* checkbox sitting at lighting-level alongside *Shadows* / *Shadow tweaks*. The preset machinery flips the per-view `lookView.atmosphereEnabled` via `ViewMan` (`CNodeView3D.js`).
- **Orbit Image Set — AZ Start and single-shot sweeps** (Export → Orbit Image Set): new *AZ Start* slider (default 0, 0–355, step 5) shifts the azimuth sweep clockwise from a chosen starting heading. Setting *AZ Step* to 0 produces a single shot per elevation at *AZ Start*; setting *EL Step* to 0 produces a single elevation at *EL Start* (and skips the 90° cap row). The shot builder normalizes *AZ Start* with a double-mod-and-add so out-of-range values wrap cleanly.
- **On-screen histogram option** (Video → Effects → *Histogram On-Screen*): new `CNodeGUIFlag` `videoHistogramOnScreen` (default off) limits histogram sampling to the pixels currently visible in the video view rather than the full source frame. `CNodeVideoHistogramView.getHistogramSourceRect()` maps the view's `dx/dy/dWidth/dHeight` back to source-pixel coordinates and `drawSample()` now takes that sub-rect as input.

### Improvements
- **Subfolder open/closed state round-trips through save/load**: `MenuBar.modSerialize` in `lil-gui-extras.js` now walks `gui.folders` recursively, capturing each subfolder's `_closed` flag keyed by `$title.innerHTML`; `modDeserialize` reapplies the map. Top-level docked menus still always close on load — only nested folder state round-trips. Folders without a matching saved entry keep their default state, and saved entries that don't match any current folder are silently dropped, so adding/renaming/removing folders never breaks loading.
- **Global menu items survive custom sitch loads**: `loginServer` (file.savingDisabled), the local-folder status row, *Add Object*, *Export Debug Log*, and the HUD Color picker now have `.perm()` applied so they persist through the lil-gui teardown that happens when a custom sitch loads (`CFileManager.js`, `index.js`, `HUDColor.js`).
- **Faster tonal adjustments**: `CNodeVideoViewFilters.applyShadowHighlight` / `applyDehaze` now use dedicated tight per-pixel loops (`applyTonalRegionLoop`, `applyShadowHighlightRegionLoop`, and float-output variants for the shadow→highlight→dehaze pipeline). The new helpers hoist slider-dependent values out of the loop, share a single luma calculation across the shadow and highlight passes, and use module-level constants (`TONAL_REGION_WIDTH`, `TONAL_REGION_INV_WIDTH`, `TONAL_LUMA_R/G/B`, `TONAL_ENDPOINT_PI`). The `edgeDetect` kernel hoists the `kernelName === 'edgeDetect'` check out of the inner pixel loop too. Behavior is unchanged (covered by a new `tests/nodes/CNodeVideoViewFilters.test.js`); just noticeably faster when scrubbing with shadows / highlights / dehaze active.

### Bug Fixes
- **Fixed Resources and Orbit Image Set disappearing after File → Open**: the perm Export folder survives sitch teardown, but lil-gui's recursive destroy tears down its non-perm children. `setupResourcesMenu()` and `setupImageSetExportMenu()` are now idempotent and re-invoked from the previously-empty `sitchChanged()` hook in `CFileManager.js`. The `ImageSetExporter` instance is preserved across reloads so user-set fields (`trackToOrbit`, `azStep`, `verticalFOV`, …) persist, and the `filesParsed` listener is registered only once to avoid handler leaks. URL-driven sitch loads were unaffected (teardown hadn't run yet).

## Version 2.62.4 (2026-05-27)

### Bug Fixes
- **Terrain flicker / mass low-Z drop during rapid camera motion** (`src/QuadTreeMap.js`, `src/QuadTreeMapTexture.js`, `src/QuadTreeTile.js`). Under a fast pan/rotate (e.g. a ~45° rotational sweep) the rendered texture cut would briefly collapse: 60+ z14 leaf tiles vanished for a single frame and the eager z1/z2/z3 globe imagery showed through, producing the visible "drops to a very low Z for a frame" flicker. Root cause was in `describeDescendantCutForView`: when reconcile recursed into a forced subtree (`forcedVisibleByRendering` / `forceFullSiblingCheck`), it walked the whole forced subtree with `camera=null` and any single empty/inactive cache leaf cascaded `renderedCovered=false` / `readyCovered=false` back up to a shallow ancestor (z9/z10/z11). `reconcileRenderedTileCut`'s partial-fallback block then suppressed the entire descendant branch for one frame. The fix changes cover semantics inside a forced subtree: a child visited only because of force trusts the force signal — cover is now evaluated against `anyRendering`/`anyReady` for that subtree rather than the strict `renderedCovered`/`readyCovered`, so empty cache siblings inside a known-rendering subtree no longer veto cover. `describeDescendantCutForView` also now returns `anyRendering` alongside `anyReady`, and reconcile uses it to suppress overlapping render branches on partial-cover paths. Supporting changes in the same diff: (1) `applyParentDataFallback` in `QuadTreeMapTexture.js` (the new shared parent-data helper extracted from the old inline subdivide path) refuses to downgrade a tile that already has its own loaded high-res non-wireframe material (`!tile.usingParentData && tile.mesh.getMap()`) — surgical reactivation passes `useParentData=true` based on the *parent's* state, so an existing-tile reactivation could otherwise stomp a freshly-installed high-res child material with the parent's downsampled material (seen as a brown/khaki rectangle flickering over an otherwise-sharp area). It also refuses tiles flagged `isDeadBranch=true` (set when a `'PlaceholderTile'` error is caught in `triggerLazyLoadIfNeeded` — Esri has no imagery for that footprint), so dead leaves aren't resurrected into another load-fail cycle. (2) `QuadTreeTile.applyMaterial` now clears `this.usingParentData = false` synchronously the moment the high-res material is assigned, closing a microtask race where the `.then(() => usingParentData = false)` in `triggerLazyLoadIfNeeded` ran a tick later and let a surgical reactivation land on a stale `usingParentData=true` and stomp the fresh material. (3) New `updateMergeCameraStability` in `QuadTreeMap.js` replaces the previous scalar-sum camera fingerprint (which aliased smooth pans as "stable" — a `(+dx, -dy, 0)` translation preserves the sum, and the rotation diagonal moves by only `~1 + 2cosθ` for small θ) with element-wise compare across rotation rows 0/1/2 of `matrixWorld`, translation, fov, and zoom. Visible merges (parent reclaims a ready child cut) are now gated behind `MERGE_CAMERA_STABLE_FRAMES = 12` frames of true stability, so the merge step can't fire mid-pan and flash a coarse parent tile. (4) `reconcileRenderedTileCut`'s partial-fallback branch no longer activates an inactive parent — it only unsuppresses an already-active parent — so reconcile can no longer undo PASS 2's camera-aware deactivation. (5) `subdivideTilesViewSpecific`'s surgical reactivation now calls `calculateTileVisibility` with the real view predicate (was `coverageSphereOnly`) and threads `useParentData=true` through `activateTile` so a child entering visibility shows instantly with the parent's downsampled texture rather than wireframe. (6) The `tile.z < 3` root-tile branch in subdivision no longer forces `visible=true` — that override was pinning every z2 globe tile into narrow look views and overdrawing the ready deep cut. Regression coverage: `tests/QuadTreeMapReconcile.test.js` gains nine new cases (force-visited empty wing siblings, coverage-invisible full rendered cover, view-mode-only parent reconcile, ready-covered inactive parent reveals suppressed children, view-ready cover not vetoed by coverage-only siblings, preload-margin children don't force fallback, surgical reactivation passes `useParentData`, merges wait for camera stability, `activateTile` applies parent-data fallback when reactivating, and a top-level "reconcile never activates an inactive parent" guard), and one prior test that encoded the old "suppress rendering descendants under coarse parent" behavior is rewritten as the new force-visited-empty-siblings case. All 48 jest tests pass; an MCP-driven 45° rotational sweep that previously dropped 13–14 z14 batches per sweep now drops zero.

---

## Version 2.62.3 (2026-05-27)

### New Features
- **Sun Reflection slider on synthetic clouds** (Objects → *Clouds: \<name\>* → Properties): a new `sunReflection` slider is added to `CNodeSynthClouds` (`src/nodes/CNodeSynthClouds.js`), range 0–10000 in steps of 1 (meters), default 0 (no-op). The control sits in the Properties subfolder alongside Density/Opacity/Brightness, uses the new `synthClouds.sunReflection.label` i18n key (`src/i18n/en.js`), and persists via `serialize()` / `static deserialize()`. Implementation adds two `ShaderMaterial` uniforms — `reflectionPoint` (vec3) and `reflectionRadius` (float) — and a per-frame `preRender(view)` update. When `sunReflection > 0`, `Globals.sunLight` exists, and `this.localUp` is set, `preRender` looks up `NodeMan.get("lookCamera", false)` and computes the specular reflection point on the locally flat cloud plane through `this.group.position` with normal `localUp`: it mirrors the lookCamera position across that plane (`camMirror = camPos − planeNormal · 2h`, where `h = (camPos − planePoint) · planeNormal`), then traces a ray from `camMirror` in the already-normalized `sunDirection` until it intersects the plane (`t = −((camMirror − planePoint) · planeNormal) / (sunDir · planeNormal)`), and writes the resulting world-space point into `reflectionPoint`. If `sunDir · planeNormal ≤ 0.001` (sun at or below the cloud horizon), the effective radius is left at 0 so the shader noops. The fragment shader now computes `planeNormal = normalize(reflectionPoint − earthCenter)`, projects `vWorldPosition − reflectionPoint` onto the plane, takes its length `pdist`, and multiplies the diffuse-lit color (`texColor * color * lighting`, BEFORE `emissive` is added) by `1 + max(0, 1 − pdist / reflectionRadius)` — so brightness is +100% at the exact reflection point and decays linearly to +0% at `reflectionRadius`. The reflection point intentionally tracks the *look* camera only (not `view.camera`), so the glint stays anchored to what the look view is pointing at even when the same cloud layer is observed from `mainView`.

### Improvements
- **URL drag/drop and paste on the sitch browser** (`src/CSitchBrowser.js`, `src/DragDropHandler.js`): the sitch browser's overlay drop zone, previously file-only, now also accepts URLs. The drop-hint label changes from *Drop files to create a new sitch* to *Drop files or URLs to create a new sitch*. The internal/external drag filter is widened from `dataTransfer.types.includes("Files")` to a new `hasExternalDropData` helper that also matches `"text/uri-list"` and `"text/plain"`, applied to `dragenter`/`dragover`/`drop`. When the drop carries no files, the handler falls back to `DragDropHandler.getDroppedText(dataTransfer)` (extracts the first non-comment line of `text/uri-list`, falling back to `text/plain`, trimmed) and pushes the URL onto a new `DragDropHandler.pendingDropTexts` queue, parallel to the existing `pendingDropFiles` queue. The custom-sitch load is now shared via `_loadCustomSitchForQueuedDrops(queuedCount)`, called whenever at least one file *or* one URL was queued. `DragDropHandler.handleDrop` (the post-load processor) drains `pendingDropTexts` after `pendingDropFiles`, dispatching each via a new `handleDroppedText(text)` method. `handleDroppedText` consolidates the `http://`/`https://` discriminator that the old `else` branch of `onDrop` did inline: URLs go to `uploadURL(text, {persistDrop: true})`, anything else goes to `uploadText(text)`. The search box (`si` in `addSearchInput`) also gains a `paste` listener: pasted text starting with `http://` or `https://` is intercepted (preventDefault + stopPropagation), pushed onto `pendingDropTexts`, and triggers the same `_loadCustomSitchForQueuedDrops(1)` flow so a pasted URL behaves identically to a dragged one.
- **War.gov PR-coded Metabunk URLs resolve without fetching the thread** (`src/MetabunkThreadUtils.js`): `resolveMetabunkThreadVideoURL` now extracts a `PR-NNN` War.gov release code from the *thread URL itself* via `extractWarGovPRCode(threadURL)` before any HTTP traffic. If a code is found, it calls `getWarGovUFODvidsIdForPrCode(prCode, fetchImpl)` against the bundled `data/WARGOV/uap-data.csv` / `uap-release001.csv` index; on a hit it routes straight to `resolveDvidsVideoURL("https://www.dvidshub.net/video/<id>", fetchImpl)`, skipping the Metabunk thread fetch entirely. Failures in that fast path are swallowed and the function falls through to the existing thread-HTML scrape, so this is a pure optimization. Covered by a new `MetabunkThreadUtils.test.js` case ("resolves PR code from the thread URL before fetching Metabunk HTML") that asserts the thread URL is never fetched when the PR code resolves, using the `dow-uap-pr072-…14909` example URL.

## Version 2.62.2 (2026-05-27)

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed a double-unescape edge case in `decodeHTMLEntities` in `src/DVIDSUtils.js` and `src/MetabunkThreadUtils.js`. The previous sequential `.replace()` chain decoded `&amp;` first, so an already-encoded payload like `&amp;lt;` collapsed to `<` instead of `&lt;` because the substituted `&` was reconsidered as the start of the next entity. Rewrote both as a single-pass `String.replace` over `/&(amp|quot|#39|lt|gt);/g` backed by a `HTML_ENTITY_MAP` lookup, so each character is visited once and double-encoded input round-trips correctly. The existing 12 tests in `DVIDSUtils.test.js` and `MetabunkThreadUtils.test.js` still pass. Closes CodeQL alerts #48 and #49 (`js/double-escaping`).

### Security
- Hardened the Metabunk host check in `isIgnoredMetabunkMP4URL` (`src/MetabunkThreadUtils.js`). The previous `parsed.hostname.endsWith("metabunk.org")` test would also match attacker-controlled lookalike hostnames such as `evilmetabunk.org`; the check now requires either an exact host match or a true `.metabunk.org` subdomain before the `/styles/` path filter is applied. Defensive hardening — no known exploit path through real upstream thread content. Closes CodeQL alert #50 (`js/incomplete-url-substring-sanitization`). Alert #47 (`js/clear-text-storage-of-sensitive-data` for an API key stored in `localStorage` by `tools/airport-arrivals/index.html`) was dismissed as won't-fix: the standalone client-side tool stores the user's own ADS-B Exchange key with no backend, which is the canonical pattern.
- Upgraded `qs` to 6.15.2 in `package-lock.json` and `tools/SitrecBridge/package-lock.json` (transitive via `express` / `body-parser` and `webpack-dev-server → express@4`), closing Dependabot alerts #132 and #133 (DoS via `TypeError` in `qs.stringify` on `null`/`undefined` entries in comma-format arrays when `encodeValuesOnly` is set; medium severity). Pulled via `npm audit fix`.
- Upgraded `uuid` to 11.1.1 via a new `overrides: { "uuid": "^11.1.1" }` block in `package.json` (transitive via `sockjs → webpack-dev-server`), closing Dependabot alert #131 (missing buffer bounds check in v3/v5/v6 when a `buf` argument is supplied; medium severity). Our only caller is `sockjs` invoking `uuid.v4()` with no arguments, so the CVE is not reachable in practice, but the patched version ships anyway. The `npm audit fix` suggestion of downgrading `webpack-dev-server` 5.x to 1.16.5 was rejected. `npm audit` now reports 0 vulnerabilities in both lockfiles.
- Upgraded `tmp` to ≥ 0.2.6 in `apps/video-viewer/package-lock.json` (transitive via `electron-builder → app-builder-lib → @malept/flatpak-bundler → tmp-promise`), closing Dependabot alert #134 (path traversal via unsanitized `prefix`/`postfix`; high severity). Pulled via `npm audit fix`; `npm audit` now reports 0 vulnerabilities in the video-viewer lockfile.

## Version 2.62.1 (2026-05-27)

### Improvements
- **Render Source Video shortcut in Video Adjustments** (Video → Video Adjustments): a second `Render Source Video` button is added at the bottom of `addFiltersToVideoNode` in `src/nodes/CNodeVideoViewFilters.js`, bound to the same instance method as the existing button in Video → Video Render & Export — `CustomManager?.videoExportManager?.exportSourceVideo()`. Both buttons share the `videoExport.renderSource.label` / `videoExport.renderSource.tooltip` i18n keys, so future changes to the source-render flow or its translations apply to both. The button is styled light-green via the project's canonical `Controller.prototype.setLabelColor("#90EE90")` extension (`src/lil-gui-extras.js`), which injects a per-controller `<style>` block with `!important` rules — the only reliable way to override lil-gui's per-element `color: var(--text-color)` on a `<button>` (earlier inline `domElement.style.backgroundColor` / `style.color` writes silently lost to lil-gui's own CSS). A companion fix in the same change makes the green styling survive controller mirroring: `setLabelColor` now records `this._labelColor = color` after injecting its style block, and `mirrorController` in `src/CustomManagerMirror.js` replays `setLabelColor(controller._labelColor)` on the mirrored controller right after copying the tooltip. The standalone right-click Video Adjustments popup (and any other mirrored panel) now shows the same label colors as the source folder, which also restores colors that were silently dropping out of mirrored copies elsewhere in the codebase — the file-manager "Saving Disabled" warning, the PTZ angle indicators, the current-sub-sitch indicator, and any other `setLabelColor`-styled control.

### Bug Fixes
- **Blurry, overlapping, and z-fighting terrain tiles**: `QuadTreeMap.reconcileTextureCut` in `src/QuadTreeMap.js` is rewritten to maintain a single per-view rendered cut for texture tiles. The previous reconcile pass could reveal a deep ready branch and then have a shallower fallback parent suppress it again in the same pass, and partial descendants could render against a fallback parent — producing z-fighting and blurry fallback imagery, especially after zoom-in followed by zoom-out and after camera `goToPoint`. The new reconcile walks texture cuts top-down, marks fallback-owned branches for the pass, and tracks ready-vs-rendering tiles with `renderSuppressedLayers` on `QuadTreeMapTexture` so descendants can finish loading without drawing over fallback parents. Stale suppression is cleared when an active parent becomes a fallback again, merges only collapse immediate active children (not the deep subtree, which was over-flattening), and coverage-visible texture leaves are kept alive when strict OBB subdivision culling would otherwise reject them (`QuadTreeCullingBounds.js`). `CNodeCamera` now refreshes subdivision after `goToPoint` and fingerprints camera matrices after `matrixWorld` updates so the reconciler sees the post-move pose on the same tick. A new 276-line regression test `tests/QuadTreeMapReconcile.test.js` covers the reveal/suppress race, the merge-immediate-children rule, and the post-`goToPoint` refresh; the OBB helper import path is normalized for Jest/Webpack and Three is deduped via a `package.json` resolution so the test runs against the same Three instance as the app.

## Version 2.62.0 (2026-05-27)

### New Features
- **Orbit Image Set: Vertical FOV control** (File → Export → Orbit Image Set): added a `Use Current Camera FOV` checkbox (default on) and a `Vertical FOV (deg)` slider (range 0.1–120, default 30) in `src/ExportImageSet.js`. When the checkbox is off, the export locks the look camera's vertical FOV to the slider value once before the orbit loop (look-camera controllers are already disabled during export) and re-pins it inside `_applyPreviewCameraPose` so other nodes (match-video-aspect, fovOverride, etc.) can't nudge it mid-shot. Both `exportImageSet` and `exportImageSetFullscreen` honor the setting; the saved/restored `savedFov` snapshot is added to the existing snapshot/restore block.
- **Orbit Image Set: JPEG output** (File → Export → Orbit Image Set): new `Use PNGs` checkbox, default off. With it off, each shot is encoded as JPEG at quality 0.9 via the renamed `canvasToImageBlob` helper (formerly `canvasToPngBlob`); with it on, PNG is preserved. Filename extension switches with format (`.jpg` / `.png`). PNG encoding was the long tail of per-shot wall time; JPEG cuts encode time ~5–20× and file size ~10–15× at imperceptible loss on terrain/sky-dominated shots.
- **Orbit Image Set: Orbit Preview mode** (File → Export → Orbit Image Set): new `Orbit Preview` checkbox repurposes the main frame slider as an index into the precomputed orbit shot list (az_steps × el_steps × time_steps). `_enterPreview` snapshots camera pose/FOV, `Sit.frames`/`aFrame`/`bFrame`, `par.frame`, `Sit.startTime`, render hooks, controls, and the look-camera controllers (disables them like the export does); `_applyPreviewFrame` runs per tick via `Globals.orbitPreviewApply` (called from `updateFrame.js` after the slider sync) and moves the camera to the current shot's az/el at the current distance, shifting `GlobalDateTimeNode.startDate` at time-step boundaries. The frame slider tints green (`#1a5d2a` track, `#2ecc71` accent) to signal the mode. `preRenderFunction` re-applies the orbit pose just before each `lookView` render, fixing a two-position flicker when `fixedTarget` moved (CNodeCamera.update runs `altAdjust` and `applyGroundTrackSwitch` directly, bypassing the disabled controllers, between the per-tick update and the GL pipeline reading `camera.matrixWorld`). Orbit Distance and Vertical FOV update the preview live; `_buildShotList` is shared by preview and export so the geometry stays in lock-step. Clicking `Export Image Set` while preview is on auto-exits preview first so the export's own snapshot/restore captures a clean pre-export state. `_exitPreview` restores everything captured at enter time, including slider styling.
- **Video tonal adjustments: Shadows, Highlights, Dehaze** (Video → Video Adjustments): three new sliders in `src/nodes/CNodeVideoViewFilters.js`, range -100 to +100, default 0, with i18n keys `videoShadows`, `videoHighlights`, `videoDehaze`. Implemented as a per-pixel pass (`applyTonalAdjustmentsToImage` → `applyTonalAdjustmentToPixel`) inserted into `CNodeVideoView.getAdjustedVideoFrameSource` between Curves and Invert. Shadows lifts/crushes darker tones using a luma-based region weight centered at 0.1; Highlights does the same for brighter tones centered at 0.9; Dehaze adds a contrast-around-airlight (220) plus saturation pass when positive, or blends toward airlight with reduced saturation when negative. Result is cached per `(image, frame, shadows:highlights:dehaze)` key on `_tonalAdjustCanvas`. Reset button in Video Adjustments resets all three. The `applyByteInvertToImage` cache now also keys on a per-effect `sourceKey` so Invert correctly re-runs when the upstream chain changes.
- **Histogram tonal-loss readout** (Video → Video Adjustments → Histogram): the live RGB histogram view (`CNodeVideoHistogramView`) now overlays a tonal-resolution stats line — `Levels NN% | N.Nb | CXX | WX:1` — showing what fraction of distinct RGB input levels survive the current adjustment chain, the effective output bit depth, total collapsed inputs across RGB, and the worst many-to-one collapse for any output level. The text tints amber when any collapse is present. A transparent hover div over the readout exposes a tooltip explaining each value. Stats are computed in `calculateTonalLossStats` using per-channel `originalSeen`, `adjustedSeen`, and reverse-map bitmaps built during the histogram pass.
- **Lines of Sight: Current LOS toggle** (Show/Hide → Current LOS): new per-LOS-display checkbox (i18n key `showHiders.currentLOS`, tooltip "Show only the current frame's line of sight"). When on, `CNodeDisplayLOS` draws a single white `Line2` for just `par.frame`'s LOS into a dedicated `currentLOSGroup`, on top of the existing per-second LOS bundle. Endpoint math (including the clip-to-sea-level path for downward-pointing LOSes on large-tile sitches) is extracted into a shared `getLineEndpoints(f)` so `recalculate()` and `updateCurrentLOS(f)` share one implementation. `showCurrentLOS` is added to `simpleSerials` so the toggle survives serialization; a new `dispose()` cleans up the group, the `Line2`, and the `LineGeometry`.

### Improvements
- **Curves editor: easier to grab handles** (Video → Video Adjustments → Curves): point and curve hit radii in `CNodeVideoCurvesView` doubled — `HIT_RADIUS` 9 → 18 and `CURVE_HIT_RADIUS` 10 → 20 — so clicking near a control point or curve segment is far more forgiving without changing the visible curve.
- **View dragging: drag-off-top to close, any view commits its new position**: in `CNodeView.onViewDragEnd`, the early-return on `!this.dockable` is moved to AFTER the new position is committed via `setFromDiv(this.div)` and AFTER a top-edge close check — so any draggable view now persists its dragged position on release (previously only dockable views did), and dragging any view above `CLOSE_OFF_TOP_PX` (-5px) hides it via `setVisible(false)`.
- **Camera controls: symmetric vertical tilt clamp**: `CameraMapControls.rotateUp` in `src/js/CameraControls.js` now clamps the camera elevation symmetrically between -89° and +89° (constants `MIN_CAMERA_ELEVATION_DEGREES` / `MAX_CAMERA_ELEVATION_DEGREES`) by computing the target elevation, clamping it, and converting back to an angle delta. Replaces the old nadir-only guard, so the camera no longer pops past the zenith and the residual angle math no longer fires a tiny rotation when fully clamped.

### Bug Fixes
- **Histogram clip indicators on inverted video**: `CNodeVideoView.calculateClippingDistribution` now feeds the "original" comparison value through `getClipComparisonValue(value, invertActive)`, which mirrors via `255 - value` when the Invert filter is on. Previously the shadow/highlight clip masks compared the inverted output against the un-inverted source and reported spurious clipping.
- **Saved GUI flags loading as off**: `CNodeGUIFlag` now coerces incoming values through `normalizeGUIFlagValue` (true / 1 / "1" / "true" → on) on construction, on user change, and during `modDeserialize`, so flags persisted as numeric or string-truthy in saved sitches restore correctly across the many filters that use it.

## Version 2.61.2 (2026-05-26)

### Improvements
- **Invert switched from CSS `invert()` slider to true byte-invert checkbox**: `videoInvert` in `addFiltersToVideoNode` (`src/nodes/CNodeVideoViewFilters.js`) is now a `CNodeGUIFlag` (boolean, default `false`, tip "Invert colors with exact 255 - byte mapping"), was a 0-1 `CNodeGUIValue`. The CSS `filter: invert(x)` path is removed from `CNodeVideoView.getAdjustedVideoFrameSource` (`src/nodes/CNodeVideoView.js`) and from `getVideoEffectsFilterString` in `CMotionAnalysis.js`. A new `applyByteInvertToImage(image, videoView, frame)` in `CNodeVideoViewFilters.js` does the inversion in an image-data pass — `data[i] = 255 - data[i]` per RGB channel, alpha untouched — onto a per-`CNodeVideoView` cached canvas (`_invertCanvas`/`_invertCtx`, allocated `willReadFrequently: true`, re-allocated only on size change) keyed on `(image, frame)` so repeat reads of the same frame return the cached canvas. Called from `getAdjustedVideoFrameSource` right after `applyCurvesToImage` and before the convolution/blur stages, so invert composes inside the rest of the pixel-data pipeline rather than being smeared onto the final CSS render. The CSS `invert(x)` blend isn't a real photographic negative at intermediate values; the byte-invert pass is.
- **Removed *Greyscale* control**: the `videoGreyscale` `CNodeGUIValue` is no longer created in `addFiltersToVideoNode`, removed from `CNodeVideoView.optionalInputs`, removed from the live `filter` string in `getAdjustedVideoFrameSource`, and removed from `getVideoEffectsFilterString` in `CMotionAnalysis.js` (so motion-analysis exports also drop the greyscale step). The corresponding `getFilterNodes` / `attachInputsToVideoNode` registrations are gone. The `videoGreyscale` i18n key in `src/i18n/en.js` is left in place (harmless — nothing reads it). *Saturate* at 0 produces the same visual result.
- **Sharpen Amount = 0 is identity and sharpen no longer ringed the frame in black** (`applyConvolution` in `CNodeVideoViewFilters.js`). The sharpen kernel was `[0, -A, 0; -A, 5A, -A; 0, -A, 0]` which sums to `A` — so at `A=0` the kernel is all-zero and the convolution wrote zero to every interior pixel (the resulting black image was then *not* merged back because of the "if all-zero, skip" guard, so what the user actually saw was a no-op… but with `A=1` the original output was already a much stronger sharpen than visually expected and lerping between them was inconsistent). New kernel `[0, -A, 0; -A, 1+4A, -A; 0, -A, 0]` sums to `1` regardless of `A`, so `A=0` is the identity kernel (pass-through) and `A=1` matches the previous "full-strength" sharpen. Two related fixes in the same function: (a) the inner colour loop is now `c < 3` (alpha is no longer convolved at all, instead of being convolved then copied back), and (b) the output buffer is allocated as `new Uint8ClampedArray(data)` (a copy of the source) instead of `new Uint8ClampedArray(data.length)` (zero-filled), so the un-touched border ring (`y=0`, `y=h-1`, `x=0`, `x=w-1`) carries the original pixels instead of being black. The post-loop "merge non-zero output back into data" hack is gone — the function now calls `ctx.putImageData(new ImageData(output, w, h), 0, 0)` directly.
- **Video Curves editor: endpoints unpinned from x=0/x=255** (`CNodeVideoCurvesView.js`). Previously `normalizePoints` pad-inserted `{x:0,y:0}` and `{x:255,y:1}` whenever those exact x values weren't present, then *forced* `deduped[0].x = 0` and `deduped[deduped.length-1].x = 255` after sort/dedupe — so any attempt to drag an endpoint inward got rewritten back to the edge on the next normalize pass, and the endpoint check (`isEndpointIndex`) was "x is literally 0 or 255". Now: pad-insert only when there are 0 or 1 points (legal degenerate cases) — a single point at x<128 pads `{255,1}` and a single point at x≥128 pads `{0,0}`. The forced-x rewrite is gone. `endpointRole(index)` identifies start/end by position-in-sorted-order (`sorted[0]`, `sorted[len-1]`), not by exact x; `isEndpointIndex` delegates to that. In `setPointFromLocal`, the start endpoint is constrained to `x ≤ neighbour-1` and the end endpoint to `x ≥ neighbour+1` (keeping monotonicity) but otherwise both can move freely in x — matching Photoshop Curves where pulling the left endpoint right clips the input shadows. Independently, the `rectContainsPoint(graphRect, …)` early-return in `onMouseDown` is now only gated on the *new-point* path; selecting/dragging an *existing* point is no longer rejected when the pointer wanders outside the graph rect, so a drag that takes a control point off the visible area no longer detaches mid-drag.
- **Reset Video Adjustments updates the displayed controls** (`addFiltersToVideoNode.reset.resetFilters`). The whole reset path was rewritten from direct `videoNode.inputs.<name>.value = …` writes to a new `resetInput(name, value)` helper that prefers `node.setValue(value)`, falls back to `node.guiEntry.setValue(value)`, and only writes `node.value = value` as a last resort. The previous direct-`.value` writes bypassed lil-gui's onChange dispatch, so the slider/checkbox/dropdown UI stayed at the pre-reset position even though the underlying value was new. As part of the same pass: the Levels reset now delegates to `videoNode.levelsView?.resetLevels()` (which is the editor's own reset path — handles the five handle positions correctly) with a fall-through to individual `resetInput` writes only if no Levels editor exists, and Curves reset delegates to `videoNode.curvesView?.resetCurve()`. The Convolution Filter dropdown also explicitly calls `convolutionFilterDropdown.setValue("none")` (was only updating `filterOptions.convolutionFilterValue`, which didn't refresh the dropdown widget). Removed the now-redundant per-input `if (videoNode.inputs.<name>)` guards — `resetInput` no-ops on missing inputs. `invert` resets to `false` (matching its new flag type) and the `greyscale` reset line is gone.
- **Empty-video Video sitch keeps the drop-zone hint pinned** (`src/DragDropHandler.js`, `src/sitch/SitVideo.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeVideoView.js`, `src/nodes/CNodeVideoWebCodecView.js`). `CDragDropHandler.addDropArea` now accepts an options object; passing `{keepTextVisibleUntilMedia: true}` sets the `keepDropZoneTextVisible` flag, which (a) starts the drop-zone visible instead of opacity-0, (b) routes `hideDropZone()` through a new `showPassiveDropZoneText()` (visible, transparent background, no pointer events, full opacity) instead of hiding the element entirely, and (c) wires a one-shot `ensureMediaImportListener()` that listens for `videoImportStarted` and `videoLoaded` events on `EventManager` and clears the persistent text the first time either fires. `onDrop` now calls `clearPersistentDropZoneText()` (which flips the flag off then calls `hideDropZone()`), so the first real drop ends the persistent state too. `CNodeVideoView.uploadFile` dispatches `videoImportStarted` at the top of the import path; `CNodeVideoWebCodecView._doUploadFile` dispatches the same event for the WebCodec path. `SitVideo.setup()` passes `{keepTextVisibleUntilMedia: this.file === ""}` — so the Video sitch *with* a preset video keeps the old fade-out behaviour, and the Video sitch *without* one keeps the hint until the user actually drops something.
- **`SitVideo` no longer force-docks the Video menu into the right sidebar**: the `addMenuToRightSidebar(videoMenu); videoMenu.mode = "SIDEBAR_RIGHT"; … applyModeStyles(videoMenu)` block in `SitVideo.setup` is removed (and the now-unused `Globals` and `addMenuToRightSidebar` imports cleaned up). The Video menu opens as a normal floating menu now, and the user can dock it themselves via the dockable-views system added in 2.61.0 if they want it pinned.

## Version 2.61.1 (2026-05-26)

### Improvements
- **Dockable text views**: extends the 2.61.0 dockable-floating-views system to text panels. `CNodeNotes` (`src/nodes/CNodeNotes.js`) now passes `v.dockable = true` (it was previously locked out because the base view sets `dockable` from `draggable`, and Notes forces `draggable = false`), and `CNodeViewText` (`src/nodes/CNodeViewText.js`) defaults `v.dockable = v.dockable ?? wasDraggable` so any draggable text view inherits dock support. Both classes now also wire an `onDragEnd` callback into their drag setup (`data.viewInstance?.onViewDragEnd?.(event, data)`), which is what runs the edge-proximity dock decision in `CNodeView`; without that wiring the dock pipeline never fired for text views in 2.61.0. The per-tick `onDrag` handlers in both classes early-return when `view.dockedSidebar` is set, so the docked layout in the sidebar isn't fought by the floating drag-position math (`CNodeViewText` additionally now calls `view.setFromDiv(view.div)` on each drag tick to keep its floating rect in sync, matching what `CNodeNotes` already did).
- **Docked text scales down to fit the sidebar**: `CNodeView` gains a `dockedTextScale` field (set to `0.8` by default on `CNodeNotes` and on `CNodeViewText` via `v.dockedTextScale = v.dockedTextScale ?? 0.8` before `super(v)`). New `applyDockedTextScale()` / `restoreDockedTextScale()` methods walk `this.div.querySelectorAll("*")`, snapshot each element's inline `style.fontSize` into `dockedTextOriginalFontSizes` (a `Map`), and rewrite each element's `style.fontSize` to `getComputedStyle(el).fontSize * dockedTextScale` in pixels. `applyDockedTextScale()` is called from `updateDockedWH()` (so the scale is applied whenever the sidebar layout updates), and `restoreDockedTextScale()` is called from `undockFromSidebar()` to put the original inline font sizes back. The apply path no-ops if there is no scale set or if a scale snapshot is already active, so non-text views are unaffected.
- **Dock state persists across sitch save/load**: `CNodeView.modSerialize` now writes `dockedSidebar: "left" | "right"` when the view is currently docked, and `modDeserialize` restores it. The restore path is order-sensitive: it reads the saved value into `restoredDockedSidebar`, calls `undockFromSidebar()` if the view is somehow already docked (defensive), applies the rest of the simple-serial deserialization, then calls `dockToSidebar(restoredDockedSidebar)` before the `setVisible(v.visible)` toggle at the bottom — the existing `this.visible = !visible; setVisible(visible)` pattern is preserved so the visibility-change side-effects still fire. Saves from 2.61.0 (no `dockedSidebar` field) load unchanged.

## Version 2.61.0 (2026-05-26)

### New Features
- **Video Levels editor** (Video Adjustments → *Levels*): a new floating editor panel (`CNodeVideoLevelsView` in `src/nodes/CNodeVideoLevelsView.js`, created from `CNodeVideoView.setupLevelsView()` with `id: "videoLevelsEditor"`, relative to the `video` view, default top-right ~30% width, free aspect) shows a per-frame luminance histogram of the current decoded frame and overlays five draggable handles for a full Photoshop-style Levels operation — *input black*, *midpoint* (gamma), *input white*, *output black*, *output white*. A *Levels* checkbox (`CNodeGUIFlag` id `videoLevels`, default off) gates the effect; a reset button on the panel and the existing *Reset Video Adjustments* helper both restore the five-slider defaults (0/1/255/0/255). The five values are stored in dedicated `CNodeConstant` inputs on `CNodeVideoView` (`videoLevelsInputBlack`, `videoLevelsMidpoint`, `videoLevelsInputWhite`, `videoLevelsOutputBlack`, `videoLevelsOutputWhite`) and serialize through the editor's `modSerialize`/`modDeserialize` under a `levels: { inputBlack, midpoint, inputWhite, outputBlack, outputWhite }` block. Pixel mapping moves into `buildLevelsLUT`/`applyLevelsToImage` (`CNodeVideoViewFilters.js`), which generate a single 256-entry LUT per change of `(inputBlack, inputWhite, midpoint, outputBlack, outputWhite)` — keyed via `_levelsLastKey` — applying `out = outBlack + ((clamp(in, inBlack, inWhite) − inBlack)/(inWhite − inBlack))^(1/midpoint) × (outWhite − outBlack)`. The old `applyLevelsMidpointToImage` is preserved as a thin wrapper that calls `applyLevelsToImage({midpoint})`, so the previously-standalone *Levels Midpoint* slider keeps working. The Levels editor and the existing Curves editor are now both opened/closed by the same Video Adjustments visibility handler (`updateVideoAdjustmentHelperVisibility` fans out to `updateLevelsVisibility` and `updateCurvesVisibility`); when both are visible at once and would overlap, `moveEditorTowardCenterIfOverlapping` nudges the newly-opened panel inward so the two never sit on top of each other.
- **Dockable floating views**: `CNodeView` (`src/nodes/CNodeView.js`) gains a `dockable` flag (defaults to `draggable`) and a complete dock/undock subsystem. On drag end, if the pointer is within `DOCK_EDGE_PX` (36 px) of the left/right window edge — or already inside the corresponding sidebar's `getBoundingClientRect()` — the view's `<div>` is re-parented into the left or right sidebar (`getLeftSidebar()`/`getRightSidebar()` from `PageStructure.js`), its absolute positioning is swapped for relative + margin (`DOCK_MARGIN_PX` = 8), resize handles are hidden, and its size is recomputed by `updateDockedWH()` to fit the sidebar's inner box while preserving the floating aspect ratio (cached at dock time as `dockedAspectRatio`). The floating rect (`leftPx/topPx/widthPx/heightPx`) is captured into `floatingRectBeforeDock` before docking and restored on undock — including a `requestAnimationFrame`-deferred pixel-size restore so the view doesn't shrink to a degenerate size during the parent swap. Dragging a docked view back out of the sidebar undocks it at the pointer's location (centred horizontally on the cursor, 16 px above the cursor vertically), clamped inside the floating parent's container. The sidebar containing a docked view auto-shows on dock (`showLeftSidebar`/`showRightSidebar`) and auto-hides when its last visible child is removed (`hideSidebarIfEmpty` — invoked on undock, on `setVisible(false)`, and on `dispose`). The drag pipeline gains `onDragStart`/`onDragEnd` callbacks so the dock decision runs only on drag release; while a view is docked, the per-tick `onDrag` returns early and `updateWH` short-circuits to `updateDockedWH` so the normal fractional-positioning path can't fight the docked layout.

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed the timeline / A-B range being stuck at stale values after a custom sitch loads a video whose length differs from the saved one (a `bFrame` of 6299 against `Sit.frames = 2410` would leave the playhead and the B-end orphaned past the real end of the video). `src/UpdateSitFrames.js` gains `lastSitFrame()` (returns `floor(Sit.frames) − 1`, never negative) and `clampSitFrameRange()`, which clamps `Sit.aFrame`, `Sit.bFrame`, `par.frame`, and `par._frameOverride` into `[0, lastSitFrame()]` and enforces `aFrame ≤ bFrame`. `updateSitFrames` now snapshots whether `bFrame` was at the *old* last frame, sets `Sit.frames = Sit.videoFrames`, snaps `bFrame` to the new end only if it was at the old end (preserving an intentional shorter range otherwise), then calls `clampSitFrameRange()`. `updateFrame` (`src/updateFrame.js`) calls `clampSitFrameRange()` at the top of every tick and drops the `?? Sit.frames-1` fallback for `B` since `bFrame` is now guaranteed valid. `CNodeFrameSlider`'s touch handlers and `updateSliderRange` switch from `Sit.frames` (one past the end) to `lastSitFrame()` (the actual last frame) for both the slider max and the touch-to-frame mapping. Covered by new `tests/UpdateSitFrames.test.js`.
- Fixed independent MISB-shaped tracks being silently retimed by whichever video happened to be loaded. `CNodeTrackFromMISB.recalculate` (`src/nodes/CNodeTrackFromMISB.js`) used to fall back to a "use real video PTS for wall-clock" path whenever the loaded video exposed real per-frame PTS but the KLV records lacked PES PTS — which pulled KML/ADS-B, CSV, STANAG, SRT, and other MISB-shaped tracks onto the video's (non-uniform) PTS timeline instead of sitch wall-clock time, so any video with dropped-frame jumps would visibly desync those tracks from the sim by the cumulative dropped-frame interval. That branch (`useRealVideoPTSForWallClock`) is removed; wall-clock mode now always uses `msStart + frameTime*1000` and only the explicit PES-PTS-per-record case (`usePESPTS`) still drives `msNow` from `videoData.framePTSus`. Covered by new `tests/nodes/CNodeTrackFromMISB.test.js`.

## Version 2.60.0 (2026-05-26)

### New Features
- **Look-camera ground-track switch** (Camera menu → *Switch to Ground Track at*): `CNodeCamera` gains a `switchToGroundTrackFrame` integer (serialized via `addSimpleSerial`) and the matching GUI slider is added only on `lookCamera` (`addGroundTrackSwitchGUI`). On every frame `f >= switchFrame > 0`, `applyGroundTrackSwitch(f)` calls `getGroundTrackSwitchTarget(switchFrame)`, which re-runs the camera's controllers at the switch frame (via `applyControllers(switchFrame)` plus the `in.altAdjust` raise), raycasts the camera's forward direction against the local ground via `raycastLocalGround` (`Raycaster` cached on the node), and caches the resulting world-space point. The live camera is then `lookAt`'d at that cached point with `getUpVector(position)` while leaving its own position untouched, so a path-following look camera now "locks onto" the ground spot it was looking at at the switch frame. Controller re-application is guarded by `_groundTrackSwitchComputing` to prevent recursion, all camera state (`position`, `quaternion`, `up`, `fov`, `near`, `far`) is saved and restored across the raycast, and the cache is invalidated on terrain reload via `onTerrainLoaded(() => clearGroundTrackSwitchCache())`. Slider range is auto-clamped to `Sit.frames - 1` in `updateGroundTrackSwitchFrameRange()` each frame, so it tracks sitch length changes. 0 disables; a one-shot console warning fires if the ground raycast misses.
- **Orbit Image Set** (File → Export, renamed from *Image Set* in `ExportImageSet.js`): the `ImageSetExporter` is significantly extended.
  - New *Track to Orbit* dropdown sourced from `targetTrackSwitch.inputs` via `buildTrackOptions`; defaults to the `"fixedTarget"` sentinel which preserves the legacy `targetObject` / `targetTrack` / `targetSphere` fallback chain in `resolveTargetPosition(trackKey)`. Any *explicit* non-default selection that fails to resolve to a node with a `.p()` method now fails closed (returns `null` from `resolveTargetPosition`) and the exporter alerts the user, instead of silently orbiting a different entity. The options list is refreshed on folder open, but only if the key signature actually changed; when it does change, the new controller's DOM row is re-inserted at its original `$children` index so lil-gui's `options()` reassignment (which destroys-and-appends) doesn't shuffle the row to the bottom.
  - Time-stepping: new *Time Step (minutes)* (default 120) and *Number of Time Steps* (default 1) controls. The shot list is built in `(time, el, az)` order; on each new `t` the exporter calls `GlobalDateTimeNode.setStartDateTime(baseStartDate + t*timeStepMinutes*60_000, true)` followed by `GlobalDateTimeNode.recalculateCascade()` so date-driven nodes like `CNodeSatelliteTrack` rebuild their arrays before the target is re-resolved (the chosen track may move with time). The original `Sit.startTime` and the DateTime node's start are restored in the `finally` block — `setStartDateTime` rewrites `Sit.startTime` via `populate()`, so `Sit.startTime = savedSitStartTime` pins it back to the original string for deserialization stability.
  - Distance control: *Use Current Camera Distance* checkbox (default on). When off, a new persistent `CNodeGUIValue` with `id: "imageSetOrbitDistance"` and `unitType: "big"` (so its slider displays NM/mi/km depending on user units, `getValueFrame(0)` returns metres) drives the orbit radius; the node's `guiEntry` is shown/hidden by the checkbox's `onChange`.
  - Defaults tweaked: *AZ Step* now defaults to 45 with stride 5 (was 10/1), *EL Step* defaults to 20 (was 10).
  - Filenames gain a `_tNN` segment when `numTimeSteps > 1` (zero-padded to the width of the largest index).

### Improvements
- **Unsupported MOV/MP4 video error now identifies the codec**: when `MP4Demuxer.onReady` finds no usable `videoTracks[0]` but `info.tracks` does contain a `type: 'video'` entry, the thrown error names the codec (with Apple ProRes detected from any of `apcn`, `apch`, `apcs`, `apco`, `ap4h`, `ap4x`), reports the video dimensions, explains that Sitrec only loads browser-decodable H.264/HEVC MP4/MOV (i.e. tracks WebCodecs can configure), and prints a ready-to-use `ffmpeg -i input.mov -c:v libx264 -pix_fmt yuv420p -c:a aac -movflags +faststart output.mp4` command. The fallback "no video track found" message now also hints at unsupported data/metadata-only files. `CVideoMp4Data.showError` for the config-rejected path is also reformatted onto two lines so the underlying message is legible (was a single run-on line). All change in `src/js/mp4-decode/mp4_demuxer.js` and `src/CVideoMp4Data.js`.

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed `CNodeDisplayTrack` wall-mesh builder asserting (or producing degenerate geometry) on tracks where some samples are missing or `undefined`. The Vector3-vs-object detection (`trackPoint.x !== undefined` → wrap as `{position: trackPoint}`) is now done *before* the `distanceTo(lastPoint)` step, and a new `if (trackPoint?.position === undefined) continue` early-out skips frames with no position entirely. Previously, the wrap/skip logic ran only inside the post-distance branch, so a missing-sample frame would either NaN-pollute `lastPoint` or crash before reaching the guard. The remaining wall-build code is unindented one level now that the `if (trackPoint?.position !== undefined)` block is gone.

### Internal
- Visual regression test runner (`tests_regression/regression.test.js`) no longer waits on the default `"No pending actions"` console marker — it only waits on an explicit `waitFor` string when one is passed. The 60s/180s/600s timeout ladder is replaced with a single 600s wait for the explicit case. This removes a 180s readiness wait that was firing on every entry without one, but still relies on `waitForSceneToSettle` (settle + idle frame budget) to gate the screenshot. No user-facing effect.

## Version 2.59.1 (2026-05-25)

### Improvements
- **Histogram toggle** (Video Adjustments): added a new *Histogram* checkbox (i18n-keyed `videoShowHistogram`, defaults on) sitting between *Levels Midpoint* and *Curves* in the Video Adjustments folder. `CNodeVideoView.updateHistogramVisibilityFromVideoAdjustments()` now ANDs the flag with the folder-open check, so the live RGB histogram panel is hidden whenever either the flag is off or Video Adjustments is closed. The flag is added to `CNodeVideoView`'s `optionalInputs` list and registered in `getFilterNodes` / `attachInputsToVideoNode` in `CNodeVideoViewFilters.js` so it serializes with custom-sitch saves; the *Reset Video Adjustments* helper restores it to `true` alongside the other defaults. The visibility check uses `!== false`, so older saves without the input still show the histogram by default.

### Bug Fixes
- Fixed a stale-frame artifact in the Curves filter: `applyCurvesToImage` in `CNodeVideoViewFilters.js` cached on `(image, revision)` only, but some decode paths reuse the same `image` object reference across frames while the underlying pixels change. The cache would then hand back the previously-adjusted canvas for the new frame. `applyCurvesToImage` now takes a `frame` argument and keys the cache on `(image, frame, revision)` via a new `_curvesLastFrame` field; `invalidateCurveResult` clears it, and the call site in `applyAdjustmentsToImage` passes the current frame.

### Internal
- Unified the two separate `guiVideoEffectsFolder.onOpenClose` handlers (one for histogram, one for curves) into a single shared listener installed by `setupVideoAdjustmentsVisibilityHandler()`, which calls a new `updateVideoAdjustmentHelperVisibility()` that fans out to `updateHistogramVisibilityFromVideoAdjustments()` and `updateCurvesVisibility()`. The standalone "Video Adjustments" context-menu (right-click) open/destroy path uses the same helper, and now also calls it once after `CustomManager.setupDynamicMirroring` so the helpers settle on the mirrored folder state. No standalone user-visible change, but this is the wiring that ties the new *Histogram* toggle into the existing show/hide logic.

## Version 2.59.0 (2026-05-25)

### New Features
- **Video curves editor** (Video → Video Adjustments → *Curves* / *Show Curves*): a Photoshop-style RGB tone curve, implemented as `CNodeVideoCurvesView` (a draggable/resizable canvas overlay positioned at the right side of the video view, `id: "videoCurveEditor"`). Two new `CNodeGUIFlag`s in `CNodeVideoViewFilters.addFiltersToVideoNode` drive it: `videoCurves` (off by default, *Curves* — gates whether the LUT is applied) and `videoShowCurves` (on by default, *Show Curves* — gates whether the editor panel is visible). Points are stored in input space (0-255) and output space (0-1) and rendered via a 256-entry `Uint8ClampedArray` LUT (`curveLUT`) regenerated whenever points change; `curveRevision` is bumped so `applyCurvesToImage` invalidates its cached canvas. `applyCurvesToImage` walks the RGBA bytes of the current frame and substitutes `lut[r]/lut[g]/lut[b]` channel-wise, so the curve is a luminance-shaping tone curve applied identically to R, G, B (not per-channel curves). The curve is inserted into the existing video adjustment pipeline in `getAdjustedVideoFrameSource` between *Levels Midpoint* and the convolution/blur stages, so it composes with Brightness/Contrast/Levels Midpoint upstream and Blur/Hue/Saturate/Invert downstream. The editor panel auto-shows when Video Adjustments is open (live folder or pinned standalone menu, see histogram entry below) AND both *Curves* and *Show Curves* are on; points serialize via `modSerialize` so curves survive saves. The *Reset* helper on the Video Adjustments folder now clears curves to identity and re-enables *Show Curves*.
- **Video histogram with clipping warning** (Video → Video Adjustments): a live RGB histogram panel (`CNodeVideoHistogramView`, `id: "videoHistogram"`) auto-appears at the top-right of the video view whenever the Video Adjustments folder is open (`isVideoAdjustmentsOpen()` returns true for either the live `guiVideoEffectsFolder` or the standalone "Video Adjustments" context/persistent menu). Each frame is downsampled to ≤256 px on the long side and the histogram is computed for both the unadjusted source and the adjustment-pipeline output, so the bins reflect what the user will actually see after Brightness/Contrast/Levels Midpoint/Curves/etc. Two clipping indicators sit in the top-left (shadow, blue arrow-down) and top-right (highlight, red arrow-up) of the panel: their fill turns colored whenever the adjusted frame contains pixels at 0 or 255 that weren't already that value in the unadjusted source (so a frame that natively has true black doesn't flag as shadow-clipped); clicking either indicator toggles a per-channel clip-warning mask overlay on the actual video pixels (shadow-clipped pixels painted solid blue, highlight-clipped pixels painted solid red) via `setClipWarningMaskEnabled` / `getClipWarningMask` on `CNodeVideoView`. The "Video Adjustments" context menu spawned by right-clicking the video was switched from auto-closing (`createStandaloneMenu(…, true)`) to persistent (`…, false`) so users can interact with both menu and panel; ESC handler is removed and the menu's `destroy` is wrapped to re-hide the histogram (and curves editor) when the menu is closed.
- **Configurable HUD color** (View → *HUD Color*): a `CNodeGUIColor` node (`id: "hudColor"`, default `#ffffff`, surfaced as `setupHUDColor(guiMenus.view)`) drives the color of all HUD-style overlays. `getHUDColor(alpha)` returns the picked color (with optional alpha suffix for `#rrggbbaa`) and `resolveHUDColor` lets call sites pass the literal string `"hud"` to opt in. Wired through: `CNodeCompassUI` (heading text, "N" letter, compass crosshair lines — with a `lastHUDColor` cache key so the redraw triggers on color changes), `CNodeMQ9UI` (HUD text/full-brightness items use `getHUDColor()`, the dimmer `#888888` grid text bucket uses `getHUDColor(0.55)`, scale lines/center N/elevation ladder/crosshair all switched), `CNodeDisplaySkyOverlay` (per-label `useHUDColor` flag — set automatically when no explicit color was supplied — controls whether the label tints by HUD color, with the per-frame transparency baked into the returned color instead of a separate alpha suffix), `CNodeLabels3D` (defaults `useHUDColor = v.useHUDColor ?? this.in.color === undefined`, so any unconfigured measurement label follows HUD color), `CNodeViewUI.renderCanvas` (resolves `"hud"` sentinel in `text.color` via `resolveHUDColor`), and the time-display widget added by `AddTimeDisplayToUI` / `SetupFromKeyAndData` (previously hardcoded `#f0f000` and `"pink"`, now `"hud"` for both labels).
- **Metabunk thread URL drag-and-drop** (`MetabunkThreadUtils.js`, hooked in `CDragDropHandler.uploadURL`): dropping a `metabunk.org/threads/...` URL now fetches the thread HTML, scans for a war.gov/UFO `PR\d{3}` reference in the thread title (resolving via `WarGovUFOUtils.getWarGovUFODvidsIdForPrCode` → DVIDS), then for embedded DVIDS/war.gov/direct-MP4 links in the message body (preferring `.message-body`/`.bbWrapper`/`article.message` so navigation/sidebar boilerplate is ignored), and finally falls back to any non-Metabunk-stylesheet `.mp4` URL. Resolved video URL is forwarded back into `uploadURL`, so the rest of the load path is unchanged. Also adds a startup `?drop=URL` query parameter (`loadStartupDropURLAfterSitchSetup` in `src/index.js`) that fires `DragDropHandler.uploadURL(dropURL, {persistDrop: false})` once the initial sitch is set up — works for both the cold-start path and the `newSitch` switch path, and is gated by a `startupDropURLLoaded` flag so it only runs once. This makes Sitrec linkable to a specific Metabunk thread / DVIDS / war.gov page.
- **war.gov UFO video URL drag-and-drop** (`WarGovUFOUtils.js`, hooked in `CDragDropHandler.uploadURL`): dropping `https://war.gov/ufo` (optionally with a `#<PR-code-or-title>` fragment) loads two CSV catalogs (`data/WARGOV/uap-data.csv`, `data/WARGOV/uap-release001.csv`) shipped with Sitrec, looks up the matching DVIDS Video ID by PR code (e.g. `PR-001` parsed via `extractWarGovPRCode`) or by normalized title (NFKD/diacritics-stripped, slug-normalized), and delegates to the DVIDS resolver. The catalog is cached in a module-level `warGovCatalogPromise` so subsequent drops don't re-fetch.
- **DVIDS video URL drag-and-drop** (`DVIDSUtils.js` + `sitrecServer/dvidsVideo.php`, hooked in `CDragDropHandler.uploadURL`): dropping a `dvidshub.net/video/...` page URL now resolves the underlying MP4 via the new PHP proxy and forwards the direct asset URL through `uploadURL`. The same code path also opens the `uploadURL` allow-list to **direct asset URLs from any host** when the URL's path extension is one of the supported media/data extensions (`mp4`/`mov`/`webm`/`avi`/`m4a`/`mp3`/`h264`/`dad`/`ts`/`m2ts`/`mts`/`kml`/`kmz`/`csv`/`json`/`srt`/`txt`/`tle`/`glb`/`ply`/`png`/`jpe?g`/`gif`/`webp`/`tiff?`) — previously the same-domain check would reject any drop from outside `SITREC_DOMAIN` / `SITREC_DEV_DOMAIN` / `amazonaws.com`.
- **A/B range source-video render** (Video → Video Render & Export → *Render Source Video*): new export that writes the raw source video between *In Frame* and *Out Frame* at its original resolution and framerate, with the current Video Adjustments pipeline (Brightness/Contrast/Levels Midpoint/Curves/Convolution/Echo/Blur/Hue/Saturate/Invert/Greyscale/Zoom+Pan) baked in. Implemented via a new `drawAdjustedSourceFrame(frame, canvas)` on `CNodeVideoView` and `getAdjustedVideoFrameSource(image, frame)` that mirrors the live render pipeline against an off-screen canvas of the original video dimensions (rotation-aware). `requestSourceFrameForExport` pre-warms each frame in the WebCodec cache before drawing, with `CVideoWebCodecBase.waitForFrame` now actively `requestGroup`-ing the missing frame group instead of waiting passively.
- **Render loops + speed/pingpong baked into video exports**: new `Loops` slider (1-20, Video → Video Render & Export) plus a `pingPong` flag on `par` (Time Settings → *In-Out Pingpong*) plus `par.playbackSpeed` are now honored by both *Render Source Video* and the existing *Render Single View Video* / *Render Viewport Video* path. `createVideoExportFramePlan` in `VideoExporter.js` builds an explicit source-frame sequence (A→B, optionally bouncing back B→A for pingpong, repeated `loops` times), clamps the output FPS at `MAX_OUTPUT_FPS`, computes a `frameStep` for higher-than-realtime playback speeds, and exposes `frameAt(i)`. The export filename now carries a speed suffix (e.g. `_2x_pingpong_3loops`). Audio is skipped (with a console note) whenever playback speed ≠ 1, pingpong is on, or loops > 1, because the export would desync.
- **In/Out frame keyboard shortcuts** (`KeyBoardHandler.js`): pressing **I** sets `Sit.aFrame` to the current frame; **O** sets `Sit.bFrame`. Each clamps the other end so `aFrame ≤ bFrame`, calls `updateFrameSlider()`, kicks `NodeMan.recalculateAllRootFirst()`, and dispatches `abFrameChanged`. The Time Settings menu labels were renamed accordingly: *A Frame* → *In Frame [I]* and *B Frame* → *Out Frame [O]* (i18n keys `dateTime.aFrame` / `dateTime.bFrame`).
- **Export Video Frame split into JPG and PNG buttons** (Video → Video Render & Export): the old single *Export Video Frame* button (always PNG) is replaced with two — *Export Video Frame (JPG)* (i18n `videoExport.exportFrameJpg`) and *Export Video Frame (PNG)* (i18n `videoExport.exportFramePng`) — by passing a format argument through `exportVideoFrame(...)`.

### Improvements
- **"Auto Tracking" renamed to "Point Track"** across the user-facing UI: the Video-menu folder is now *Point Track* (was *Auto Tracking*, `CObjectTracking.addObjectTrackingMenu` in `src/CObjectTracking.js`), the `tracking.*` i18n labels switch to *Enable / Disable Point Track*, *Start / Stop Point Track*, with tooltips reworded ("point track" / "point-track" cursor), and the JetLOS LOS-source dropdown option is renamed from *Camera + Auto Track* to *Camera + Point Track* (`CustomManagerSetup.setupMethods`). German locale (`src/i18n/de.js`) gets the matching *Punkt-Track aktivieren / deaktivieren / starten / stoppen* labels. Internal identifiers (`CObjectTracking`, `CNodeAutoTrackLOS`, the `autoTracking` serialization namespace, the `MISBValueDecoders` ST 0601 published-spec field name *Auto-Track*) are intentionally **not** renamed — the rename is presentation-only. Legacy custom saves are migrated in `CustomManagerSerialize.serializeMethods`: `mods.JetLOS.choice === "Camera + Auto Track"` is rewritten to `"Camera + Point Track"` before `modDeserialize` runs, so saved sitches still resolve to the renamed switch input.
- **Help menu reorganization** (`src/index.js` `initializeOnce`): a new top-level *What's New* link sits directly under the Help menu (i18n key `menus.help.whatsNew`), pointing at the concise end-user release notes (`docs/WhatsNew.md`). The detailed engineering changelog moved to a separate file (`docs/WhatsNew-Details.md`) and now lives inside Help → Documentation as *What's New (Details)* (i18n key `menus.help.documentation.whatsNewDetails`). The two-file split (concise vs. detailed) is now formalized — see the header comment in each file and `.claude/agents/whatsnew-accuracy.md` for the rules. Translations for both new keys are present across all 10 shipped locales (de/en/es/fr/it/ja/nl/pt/ru/zh).
- **High-altitude measure labels use large units**: `CNodeMeasureAB` (`src/nodes/CNodeLabels3D.js`) now early-returns a label of the form `<value> <big-unit> msl` with 2 decimals whenever the measured A-point's MSL altitude is ≥ 30,480 m (= 100,000 ft, `HIGH_ALTITUDE_LABEL_THRESHOLD_M`), via `Units.withUnits(alt, 2, "big")`. Stops the measure-arrow MSL readout from drowning in 6-digit metres / 100,000-ft numbers when measuring satellite altitudes.
- **Double-click slider label to reset to default** (`src/lil-gui-slider-settings.js`): the patched `NumberController._initSlider` now captures the controller's value at construction time as `_defaultValue` and adds a `dblclick` handler on `this.$name` that calls `setValue(this._defaultValue)`. Pairs with the existing right-click-to-reset on the slider itself.

## Version 2.58.0 (2026-05-24)

### New Features
- **Physically based aerial perspective** (Effects → Atmosphere): the look view now composites distant geometry as `scene * T + sky_ray_color * (1 - T)`, where `T` is per-ray transmittance through an exponential atmosphere and `sky_ray_color` is the same per-ray sky gradient that paints the background. Distant buildings and terrain converge toward the color of the sky directly behind them instead of a fixed grey fog tint, so a city at the horizon under a yellow sunset goes yellow, under a blue overhead it goes blue. The *Atmo Visibility* slider is now mapped to physical extinction via Koschmieder's formula (`beta = 3.912 / visibility_meters`), and a normalized ray-distance prepass replaces hardware perspective depth so Earth-scale far planes do not collapse the precision available for haze integration. Surface-level, high-altitude, and near-space / satellite views all use the same height-integrated optical path through an exponential atmosphere with Rayleigh + aerosol scale-height sampling and a tangent / limb haze boost so long horizontal sightlines from space accumulate the dense lower atmosphere without an extra pass, while vertical-up sightlines correctly thin out. A continuous angle weight replaces the prior hard `slantViewT` threshold near 364,354 ft so angled-down high-altitude views no longer pop. An analytic Earth-surface/horizon air-mass fallback covers shallow sightlines into 3D Tiles where the lightweight depth prepass under-reports custom-positioned tile geometry. The feature is gated to lookView + Atmosphere + (*Horizon Haze* or *Sky Gradient*) and stays a no-op for IR/XR/legacy day-sky paths; the shader and distance render target are released when disabled. See `docs/AtmosphericAerialPerspective.md` for the full physical basis, controls, and limits.
- **Atmosphere horizon haze gradient** (Effects → Atmosphere): independent per-ray horizon haze gradient rendered in `CNodeView3D` and driven by the existing `CNodeSunlight.calculateHazeColors()` solar geometry — distinct from the legacy uniform-fog horizon and used as the sky-side input to the new aerial-perspective composite above. Two new Effects-menu checkboxes wire it up: *Horizon Haze* (`view3d.atmosphereHaze`) — fog asymptote uses the pale horizon color rather than zenith blue — and *Sky Gradient* (`view3d.skyGradient`) — sky fades from zenith blue to horizon haze. Also adds *Allow Sky Gradient on mobile* (off by default).
- **Terrain self-shadowing**: with `Globals.shadowsEnabled` and `Globals.terrainReceivesShadow` both set, `QuadTreeTile.buildMesh` now writes `mesh.castShadow = mesh.receiveShadow = true` (was caster=false, receiver-only) and calls `markShadowCastersDirty` so the shadow map repopulates on the next pass. `CNodeTerrain.refreshShadowFlags` mirrors the change for loaded tiles, tracks caster-state transitions, and sweeps skirt meshes back to non-caster/non-receiver via the active texture map (`this.maps?.[this.UI?.mapType]?.map`) — skirts stay off because their degenerate UVs/normals would otherwise produce seam artifacts. Paired with the new default `terrainReceivesShadow = true` (see Lighting reorg below), ridges, hills, and crater walls now darken their lee when sun shadows are on.

### Improvements
- **Lighting menu master shadows toggle + Shadow tweaks reorganization + defaults**: `CNodeLighting` gains a `shadowsEnabled` field (default off, serialized) backing a new top-level *Shadows* checkbox at the top of the Lighting menu. `CNodeView3D.areShadowsEffective()` now requires that master AND the per-view flag, so existing/imported sitches that enable shadows per-view stay quiet until the master is on. Per-view *Shadows in Main View* / *Shadows in Look View* (their per-view `shadowsEnabled` default flipped false → true) and *Terrain receives shadows* now live inside the *Shadow tweaks* subfolder alongside the sliders, ordered per-view → terrain → tunables. Defaults: `terrainReceivesShadow` flips false → true (paired with the new terrain self-shadowing); `shadowRadius` default drops from 1000 m to 300 m, and its label is renamed from "Shadow radius (m)" to "Shadow min radius (m)" with a new tooltip explaining the orthographic frustum auto-fits to FOV × distance-to-look-at-ground and this slider is the floor when a tight view would otherwise produce a smaller extent. Locale labels/tooltips updated to match.
- **Stabilized video exports can embed the Video Info Display**: a new *Include Video Info Display* checkbox in the Auto Tracking menu (i18n key `tracking.includeVideoInfo`, off by default) composites the on-screen Video Info Display readouts (frame counter, timecode, dates) AND any visible OSD Tracker data series into "Render Stabilized Video" and "Render Stabilized Expanded" exports. `CObjectTracking.renderStabilizedVideo` now calls `videoInfo.drawInfoToContext(compositeCtx, …)` per frame when the flag is on, so a stabilized clip dropped into another tool keeps its provenance. `CNodeVideoInfoUI` was simplified at the same time — ~130 lines of redundant per-instance rendering scaffolding collapsed onto the shared draw-to-context path now used by both the live UI and the export.

### Bug Fixes
- **Motion analysis (and the panorama generation that consumes it) no longer drifts on videos containing true duplicate frames** (e.g. 30 fps re-encodes of 15 fps source). `MotionAnalyzer` gained a `skipDuplicateFrames` param (default on, surfaced as *Skip Duplicates* in the Motion Analysis menu, i18n key `motionAnalysis.params.skipDuplicateFrames`) backed by `detectDuplicateFrame` / `compareGrayForDuplicate`, which sample consecutive grayscale frames at stride for identical-pixel ratio ≥ 0.93 and mean abs diff ≤ 0.15. A pre-pass `buildDuplicateFrameMap` runs before motion analysis and caches a verdict per frame; the analyzer returns a zero-motion result (and `getPriorAnalysisFrame` skips duplicates when picking the prev-context frame for optical flow) so panorama stitching no longer accumulates spurious motion vectors across identical frames. The analyze progress UI now reports three steps — *Detecting duplicate frames*, *Analyzing motion*, *Filling motion gaps*.
- **Analyze All Frames no longer deadlocks at "Analyzing 100%"**: the old loop set `par.paused = false` and trusted the play loop to walk frames into the cache, then waited on `isCacheFull()`. When the playhead sat past `bFrame`, or the first `skipFrames` frames of the range needed prev-context frames outside the range, those frames never completed — `isCacheFull()` returned false forever while the progress percent rounded to 100. Two concurrent clicks (e.g. both panorama-export menu items) raced through `par.frame` / `Globals.justVideoAnalysis` with no `try/finally`, leaving the page wedged after a failure. `analyzeAllFrames` now actively walks the range, preloads `skipFrames` worth of prev-context, retries incomplete frames once the buffer is warm, reports progress as `complete-in-range / total`, coalesces concurrent callers onto a single pass via a module-level lock, and restores `par.paused` / `par.frame` / `Globals.justVideoAnalysis` in `finally`.
- **High-altitude aerial-perspective transitions fixed; Lighting "Atmosphere" toggle renamed to "Daylight Sky"**: the daytime atmosphere haze now strictly follows the renamed *Daylight Sky* toggle (i18n key `lighting.daylightSky`, was `lighting.atmosphere`); label and tooltip updated across all 10 shipped locales. Decoupled `Atmo Visibility` from depth encoding — visibility now controls extinction only — fixing a non-monotonic response in the "weird above 5000" sitch where 1 km visibility looked mostly clear and fog peaked around 62 km. Sky pixels are now traced through the same line-of-sight atmosphere model as the scene (out to top-of-atmosphere), so long slant/satellite rays integrate the dense lower atmosphere without an extra pass. The hard `slantViewT` threshold near 364,354 ft was replaced with a continuous angle weight in `CNodeView3D`, eliminating the pop in angled-down high-altitude views around the transition altitude while keeping horizontal long-distance attenuation. The follow-up commit 8ce412f8 added the Rayleigh/aerosol scale-height integration and dark-sky-radiance vs. finite-path-airlight separation that makes the same model behave correctly at orbital altitudes (the initial 4acbe439 composite still applied surface extinction to raw geometric distance, treating a vacuum sightline from a satellite as dense surface air).
- **V/B measure-arrow keys snap to the cursor again**: commit `3bd22b4c` had moved the per-pointermove terrain raycast behind an explicit "only refresh `cursorSprite.position` when something reads it" gate. The gate listed C/X/L (position-LLA snap keys) but missed V/B, which `CameraControls.updateMeasureArrow` reads every frame from `getCursorPositionFromTopView()`. Holding V or B stopped moving the endpoint with the cursor — it snapped once to whatever was cached on mouseDown and stayed there. V/B are now in the gate in `CNodeView3DMouse.onMouseMove` and `_scheduleDeferredCursorRefresh`, and in the keydown-press immediate refresh in `mouseMoveView.js` so a V/B tap with no mouse motion still snaps.
- **Legacy custom saves restored — orphan track-mod remap + ObjectTilt front-pointing**: pre-2026 custom sitches (e.g. JACKSON LLA CURVE from 2025-05) were loading with a giant opaque green sphere and a ship model pitched 90° forward. Two root causes. (a) `CustomManagerSerialize.remapLegacyTrackMods` now has an orphan track-root heuristic: legacy saves predate the stable `shortName` metadata added in `a3206292`, so mod keys like `<X>_ob` and `TrackDisplay_<X>` referenced whatever short name the parser-of-the-day produced. After the switch-based pass, mod keys are scanned for the unique `<X>_ob` marker, a single orphan root is paired with a single unclaimed current track via `track.menuText`, and only mod keys whose substituted form resolves to an existing node are rewritten — so a short root like "null" cannot collide with unrelated keys and the sphere recovers its saved size/opacity/color. (b) `CNodeControllerObjectTilt.apply` no longer calls a redundant `lookAt(next)` in the `frontpointing` and `frontpointingwind` branches. Commit `fcff41ea` ("use local up, not EUS up") added an unconditional pre-switch `lookAt` bracketed by `object.position` copy/restore, but those two branches still aimed from `oldPos` instead of `currentPos` afterwards — when ground clamping nudged the LCS above water, `(next - oldPos)` became near-vertical and tipped the ship 90° forward. The pre-switch `lookAt` already wind-corrects via `getValueFrame(f, trackPos)`, so the wind branch needed no replacement.

## Version 2.57.1 (2026-05-21)

### Bug Fixes
- **Shadows no longer vanish at long distance**: the shadow frustum was anchored at `camera.position` (despite the comment claiming it tracked the ground point) and clamped to a 1 km radius, so any visible casters more than ~1 km from the camera's nadir fell outside the frustum and silently produced no shadow when the camera climbed above ~6000 ft. The anchor now solves the camera-forward ray against a sphere of the geocentric ellipsoid radius at the camera's own latitude (not the equatorial radius, which sits ~4.5 km above mid-latitude ground and put any near-surface camera mathematically inside it). Extent auto-fits the visible footprint with `shadowRadius` acting as the floor instead of the ceiling, so the system works seamlessly from walking-street up to ISS-altitude / 0.1° FOV.
- **Shadows render at street level**: at heights near WGS84 zero (geoid undulation puts real ground a few metres below the ellipsoid in many regions, so a walking-around camera commonly has HAE ≈ -1 m), the previous code fell through from the negative near-root to the far-root of the ray-ellipsoid intersection — a point ~12 700 km away through the opposite side of the planet — and the extent saturated to the 100 km cap, blacking out the scene. The far root is now rejected entirely; when the near root is negative the anchor stays at the camera and the `shadowRadius` floor builds a sane local frustum.
- **Zoom-to-ground stops at the actual ground, not a phantom 300 m above it**: two related root causes. (a) When the mouse-to-ground raycast missed the terrain mesh, the controls and synth-object code all fell back to a sphere of `Globals.equatorRadius`; at mid-latitudes a near-surface camera is mathematically inside that sphere, so `Ray.intersectSphere` returned the far-side exit point ~1900 km through the planet, which then became `controls.target`. The fallback now uses the local geocentric ellipsoid radius and only accepts the near positive root. (b) The QuadTree subdivision pass's surgical reactivation skipped any child at `z<3` — a rule meant only for texture maps (where `calculateTileVisibility` force-returns `visible=true` with `SSE=infinity` for `z<3` to keep root tiles loaded as resample fallbacks). Elevation maps get no such force-root, so the skip stranded `mainView` at zoom-0 whenever another view had created the z=1/2 children first, and the resulting zoom-0 elevation interpolation baked +173 m into Santa Monica terrain vertices, producing a phantom ground at HAE +310 m. The skip is now gated on `isTextureMap` so each view can independently subdivide elevation down to its needed LOD.

## Version 2.57.0 (2026-05-21)

### New Features
- **3D Buildings: optional per-view cast shadows + building material modes**: independent shadow toggles for `mainView` and `lookView` (Lighting menu → *Shadows in Main View* / *Shadows in Look View*), each with its own `DirectionalLight` and shadow map so the two views render with their own LODs and casters instead of stomping on a shared shadow camera. A new *Shadow tweaks* subfolder exposes shadow map size, shadow radius (50 m – 5000 m), shadow bias, normal bias, and update throttle. *Terrain receives shadows* toggle gates whether the ground darkens under buildings. Buildings get a new *Building Material* dropdown — **Photo** (original Google/OSM textures), **Flat** (single user-picked colour, no textures), and **Half-photo** (blend) — so users can trade photo realism for clean shadow geometry. Default is off and the existing no-shadows render path is unchanged. Synthetic buildings cast shadows alongside Google Photorealistic and Cesium OSM tiles.

### Improvements
